PNC lets businesses accept USDC without keeping the crypto

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Eligible corporate clients can accept Ethereum USDC payments and receive dollars in their PNC accounts. The limited Coinbase-powered pilot keeps the banking relationship intact.

PNC's incoming stablecoin pilot gives businesses a way to accept USDC without keeping their receipts in crypto. Under the limited program announced Nov. 9, eligible Corporate & Institutional Banking clients receive USDC on Ethereum, with payments automatically converted 1-to-1 into dollars and deposited into a designated PNC account.

The payment travels over stablecoin infrastructure, but the receiving customer's balance ends up at the bank. That design keeps the deposit account in the payment relationship, even as the way of paying changes.

Coinbase supplies the underlying payment infrastructure through its Payment APIs, while PNC provides banking and treasury-management services.

The offering is a route for incoming USDC to become dollars in a banking relationship. It does not announce a PNC-issued stablecoin or put customer deposits on a blockchain.

Announced Nov. 9, 2026: PNC's limited pilot converts eligible corporate and institutional clients' incoming USDC into dollar deposits.

Dollar conversion alone does not reveal whether tokens are redeemed or transferred. The announcements do not identify either mechanism.

The disclosed PNC deposit endpoint is one part of the picture. Changes in payer and conversion balances, issuer bank deposits and reserve allocation determine the wider effect.

The customer's settlement destination is disclosed. Token supply and banking-system funding effects are separate questions.

Limited eligible-client pilot. Conversion counterparty, redemption versus inventory route, timing, fees, usage and measured deposit effects are undisclosed. Dashed paths illustrate unknown mechanisms or information needed for analysis, not verified PNC fund flows. Fed material is staff analysis, not PNC outcome data.

Qualitative route diagram from the November 9 provider announcements. Arrows in the main view show the announced customer sequence, not timing or volume. Selectable views distinguish unresolved token disposition from systemwide funding analysis. No calculations or quantitative charts.

Limited eligible-client pilot. Conversion counterparty, redemption versus inventory route, timing, fees, usage and measured deposit effects are undisclosed. Dashed paths illustrate unknown mechanisms or information needed for analysis, not verified PNC fund flows. Fed material is staff analysis, not PNC outcome data.

CryptoSlate's September coverage of the Coinbase and Moov banking partnership left institution-specific fiat settlement routes unresolved. PNC names the receiving account for its own pilot, answering a practical question for a corporate treasury: where do the proceeds arrive after someone pays in stablecoins?

Thanks for signing up to The Catalyst. Look out for your first daily email. If you already receive it, you’re all set.

For banks, that design fits an adaptation strategy described by Federal Reserve staff: respond to new payment competition through products, infrastructure and partnerships while continuing to serve customers. The pilot puts that approach into a specific receiving-payment service.

A credit in the receiving customer's PNC account tells us where that customer's money arrives. It does not tell us how the payer acquired USDC, who supplied the conversion dollars, or which other balances changed along the way. Those offsetting movements matter to whether the banking system gains funding overall.

A March 30 Federal Reserve staff model illustrates a possible inventory route. In its hypothetical cross-border payment, a recipient can sell stablecoins and deposit local currency while an intermediary retains the tokens. Fees and delays in exchanging tokens for currency can influence how much inventory intermediaries hold.

In a May 1 analysis of banks' responses to stablecoins, Fed staff distinguished aggregate deposit levels from their composition. Money can return to banks through issuers holding reserve deposits or through securities dealers redepositing proceeds. Stablecoin activity can shift the ownership and location of deposits without necessarily reducing the banking system's total deposits one-for-one.

The receiving customer's conversion is also separate from the issuer-side reserve choices discussed in CryptoSlate's October coverage.

For a business, the practical test is how efficiently those receipts become usable cash. PNC and Coinbase describe faster access to funds and greater cross-border efficiency as intended benefits. Their November announcements leave the precise settlement deadline, spendable-funds availability, operating hours, restrictions and pilot fees unspecified.

Those terms matter when comparing a blockchain transfer with the time it takes for the resulting bank balance to become available to spend. The companies have also disclosed no participating-client count, processed transaction volume or measured deposit effect in these announcements.

PNC has specified the customer's destination: a dollar deposit. The next evidence to watch is whether eligible businesses use the route and how it performs for their treasury operations.

Google Home update fixes Nest ‘Live View’ issues, improves Nest Hub and Gemini for speakers

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

A new Google Home update is rolling out with a fix for “Live View” issues affecting Nest Cam devices, improvements for the Nest Hub, upgrades for Gemini, and more.

As per usual, the latest Google Home update can be split up into a few categories, starting with Gemini. Still in early access, enrolled users can expect further improvements to smart home controls, faster timers, and “more conversational voice feedback,” as well as faster Broadcasts.

Most notable on the Gemini front is improvements to Nest Hubs, with smart displays now showing “relevant images when helpful” when you ask questions.

Visual answers with images on smart displays: On-screen answers on smart displays now will include relevant images when helpful so you can take in information at a glance.

In the Google Home app, Google is adding a “Google Home Premium” page to the settings menu, an “Eco Mode Everywhere” toggle for homes with multiple Nest Thermostats, and support for Home keys in Google Wallet for compatible locks.

More software updates are rolling out for older Nest Cam devices, including a fix for the “Live View” issue that’s been in place on some devices such as Nest Hello. You can also remove an unmigrated Nest Hello from the Home app even if it is offline. Other camera updates in this update include:

On the AI agent front, Google Home MCP connections now allow agents to control automations as well as accessing new controls and status checks across “thermostats, fans, air purifiers, robot vacuums, lighting effects, kitchen appliances, EV chargers, and smoke and carbon monoxide alarms.”

Google is also rolling out smart speaker updates right now, but details on what’s new are limited.

Ho rivisto le partite, dovevo essere più incisivo, fare scelte migliori, gestire meglio certi sforzi. E l'intesa con gli altri deve ancora crescere. L'esordio ai Mondiali contro il Senegal? Zoe Saldaña And Gwyneth Paltrow Just Bonded Over How Tough Marvel Sets Are: 'Literally The CIA'

Gwyneth Paltrow and Zoe Saldaña are both alums of the MCU now, so they can spill the tea, and boy, did they. While being part of one of the upcoming Marvel movies is certainly one of the big honors in Hollywood, there are some unique stresses that come with being part of the MCU. That's what the two actresses bonded over too, as they discussed just how secretive the world of Marvel is.

Paltrow has famously admitted she hasn’t watched any of the Marvel movies. Once she even forgot she was in a Spider-Man movie, and when asked if she was in Avengers: Doomsday recently, she responded with, “What’s that?” When Saldaña joined Paltrow on her new podcast, of course the Marvel movies came up. The conversation led to the Guardians of the Galaxy star recalling her memory of being with the Pepper Potts actress on the set of Avengers: Endgame. As she shared:

We were hanging out on the set of Avengers, you and I, just talking about life, and I remember you were like, ‘What are we doing?’ ‘Where are we?’ It felt like the world of Marvel was happening so fast, and your beautiful heart just couldn’t keep up. You were like, ‘I just can’t keep up. What’s the plot? What are we after? Who’s dead? Who’s alive?’ It was just hilarious.

Pepper Potts made her return to the MCU in Endgame for the big battle scene and to unfortunately see Tony Stark die while saving the world. Saldaña explained how clueless her co-star seemed on the set of Endgame before Paltrow went on to playfully defend herself with these words:

Well, first of all, I hadn’t seen any of the movies… you can’t read the script because they are literally the CIA, and there’s password protection and names in the script are different from the person who ends up [on screen]. Like, you know what? I actually don’t care, OK? Just tell me where to stand. Give me some context.

I mean, I think she has a point here. She’s an actor there to do her job, and there’s a lot of secrecy involved that makes it more difficult for her to do that. Much of the time, the actors aren’t trusted with a script, which is the most basic tool one usually needs to do their job.

When Gwyneth Paltrow signed up for Iron Man, it was the first movie in the MCU ever, and she said that experience was like “doing an independent film”. Endgame was filmed about a decade later, when the superhero franchise had turned into a monster success. Saldaña then spoke about how seeing Paltrow on set affected her, saying this on The Goop Guide:

You inspired me. I just watched you walk away and finish your day, and I went, ‘Yep, that’s going to be me.’ And I can’t wait. Here I was with three kids under the age of four, bad cold, trying to memorize all these scripts to then have everyone tell me the morning of ‘these are not the lines you’re going to say.’ And, [I'm] like ‘What the F!’

Yeah, this sounds frustrating. Marvel may be like the CIA, but ultimately it’s for a good reason. The filmmakers and crew want to preserve the experience for the audiences, and locking up the script of Endgame led to one of the most rewarding moments in theaters in recent years.

I love seeing these two movie stars get real about what it’s actually like to be in a Marvel movie. And you can see them in Avengers: Endgame - Encore all over again with a Disney+ subscription this Friday, November 13.

‘I’ve been a Democrat my whole life’: Alex Karp suddenly wants you to know that Palantir is progressive

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

It was only a matter of time until tech CEOs rethought their alliances with the Trump administration and pivoted back to the left now that the blue wave has swept the midterm elections.

But while many had their eyes on larger tech figures closer to the president, like Meta’s Mark Zuckerberg or on-again-off-again presidential ally Elon Musk, Palantir CEO Alex Karp beat them to the punch.

In a sit-down interview with Politico, Karp soft-launched his avowed support for the Democrats, and voiced concern over the current administration’s policies.

“I’ve been a Democrat my whole life. I grew up in a far-left environment. I know more about being a progressive than almost anybody on the planet, and I absolutely believe Palantir—all of our products are essentially progressive,” Karp told Politico.

While in the past, Karp has focused on supporting the military industrial complex through technology, he turned his focus onto economic injustice, suggesting Democrats have an advantage over Republicans when it comes to developing a solution.

“I’m really not pitching Palantir. I’m actually pitching, ‘What are you gonna do about economic justice in this country?’,” Karp said. “For many Republicans, there’s an orthodoxy that capitalism solves all problems. Maybe that was once true. I never thought it was true. It’s definitely not true now.

Past controversies, lucrative Trump-friendly contracts

The statement has undoubtedly raised some eyebrows on social media, particularly as the technology company—which Karp once referred to as “anti-woke“—has gained heat in the past years for its lucrative contracts with the Trump administration.

Perhaps its most notable—and controversial—is the $30 million contract for ImmigrationOS, a software made to help ICE track and deport undocumented immigrants. Karp addressed the controversy during the interview, commenting on trump’s approach to immigration enforcement.

“I was really happy to hear the president be honest that—you know, his words, I believe, were something like, ‘Yeah, we kinda went too far,’” Karp continued in the interview. “And I’m glad he’s saying in public now what I’ve been saying in private.”

Karp’s donation history is actually a mixed bag. This year, he donated $100,000 to Americans for Security PAC, a conservative political action committee, according to Open Secrets. But his individual candidate donations show that he has financially supported Republican Representative Jay Obernolte, as well as Democratic Senator Mark Warner and the Democratic Party of Virginia.

“My giving is equal Democrat, Republican,” Karp in the interview. “I can tell you, without going into things I’m not gonna disclose, I’m very balanced, and everyone in the political arena knows it. And I’ll tell you what, I’m also not the village idiot.”

“Pure survival grift”

But still, social media users aren’t quite to buying it.

Some have taken the interview’s revelations as fodder for memes, using AI to transform Karp into a woke figure: pink-colored hair, piercings, and pronouns in sight.

But for many others, the contradictory messaging reads as opportunistic.

“Karp spent years selling surveillance and control tools to the regime, then flips the second the midterms hit. Pure survival grift,” a user said on X.

Another added, “the back peddling gonna be legendary with every passing week that trump gets even less popular.”

Rand Fishkin’s Seattle game studio serves up first trailer for ‘The Snackbar at the End of the World’

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

by Kurt Schlosser on Nov 11, 2026 at 11:21 amNovember 11, 2026 at 11:21 am

If the eventual release of “The Snackbar at the End of the World” is a full-on meal for video game fans, news this week of the game’s first trailer should at least suffice as an appetizer.

Seattle-based Snackbar Studio, co-founded by SparkToro founder Rand Fishkin and his wife, writer Geraldine DeRuiter, unveiled its debut title and Steam page on Tuesday. The hand-drawn narrative adventure RPG puts players in the role of a chef trying to save a struggling family restaurant in a magical version of 1960s Italy.

The studio now plans to launch the game late next year, after originally targeting the second half of 2026. Snackbar raised $2.15 million from 38 angel investors two years ago, and the project began four years ago, according to Fishkin. The team includes five co-founders (Fishkin, DeRuiter, game designer Nicolas Kraj, lead programmer Miriam Cabrera and art director Francesco Mazza) plus about 10 contractors.

In the studio’s description on Steam, players forage for ingredients in the “forbidden wilds” outside town, battling explosive tomatoes, chaotic zucchini and vendetta-driven poultry. They then cook dishes back at the restaurant before a tyrannical mayor known as the Sindaco can shut it down. The game mixes combat and stealth with cooking mini-games based on real-world recipes.

The trailer is set to “’O surdato ’nnammurato,” a classic Neapolitan song performed by professional musicians recruited by the studio’s music director. Fishkin said he and DeRuiter both cried the first time they heard it.

Fishkin is best known in Seattle tech circles as co-founder of SEO software company Moz and audience research startup SparkToro, and he more recently launched Alertmouse, a tool for tracking online mentions. DeRuiter, a writer and author, is Snackbar’s head writer.

In a LinkedIn post this week, Fishkin recalled telling DeRuiter during the COVID lockdown that he wanted to fulfill a childhood dream of making a video game. Her reply: “Don’t wait. Do it now. I’ll do it with you.”

In the same post, he said the game’s themes include found family and fighting fascism “even when there’s no hope you’ll win.” He called it a love letter to DeRuiter’s small family village in the mountains outside Naples, to that era of Italy, to video games, and to the many people both of their families have lost in recent years.

Moments like hearing that song come often in the work, Fishkin said, such as when a new feature clicks or a new animation brings DeRuiter’s characters to life. He has always loved art without being good at any form of it. “I may not be able to create art, but I can enable it,” he said. “What a wonderful feeling.”

There have been lows, too. The team spent its initial 18 months on the project with a contract agency that didn’t work out, losing money with nothing to show for it. Fishkin is glad they kept going, “even when it felt financially irresponsible.”

The game is entirely human-made, and Snackbar’s no-AI stance is firm. “Geraldine hates AI with the fury of a thousand suns,” Fishkin said. He once suggested, just once, that she use Google’s AI Mode to find a synonym, and she was mad at him for the rest of a six-hour flight home from Philadelphia. Even if players, the rest of the team and the games media were fine with AI (and they’re not), DeRuiter alone would make it a no-go, he said.

Still ahead are 10 to 12 months of finishing the game’s content, playtesting, bug fixing, marketing and launch prep. Fishkin called it daunting, but said team members have done it many times before, “even though it’s our first rodeo.”

Fishkin is still juggling Snackbar with SparkToro and Alertmouse, and describes his role at the studio as mostly oversight and management. SparkToro is growing again, and Alertmouse recently hit a big milestone in annual renewals with high retention. He and his Alertmouse co-founders are putting more money into the company to fund growth.

Balancing the three roles is a challenge, he said, but only rarely completely overwhelming. “Like this week.”

In just a few weeks, AWS re:Invent brings advanced and expert-level sessions on the services you build on every day, from serverless and containers to databases and developer tools. Go hands-on with new launches the same week they’re announced, and hear directly from the engineers who built them. Experience the depth you won’t find anywhere else.

real motors brings futuristic project: ARES electric motorcycle to life with new prototypes

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

with sculptural bodywork and an analog-inspired experience, project: ARES is preparing for its first 250 production units.

South Carolina-based Real Motors has brought its futuristic electric café racer, Project: ARES, onto the road, with new working prototypes and a limited production run planned for 2027. First introduced in 2024, the motorcycle attracted attention for its sculptural bodywork and sci-fi influences. Two years later, the company has opened preorders for its first 250 units, while a recent independent test ride offers a closer look at how the machine actually performs.

The latest prototypes retain the distinctive proportions of the original design, with broad body panels wrapping around the electric drivetrain and a sharply tapered seat extending over the rear wheel. In bright red, the 03 prototype takes on the appearance of a futuristic racing machine, complete with oversized white graphics and a rectangular headlight. Its front forks are concealed beneath prominent aerodynamic covers, giving the motorcycle a particularly solid presence from the front.

Another prototype by Real Motors, numbered 04 and nicknamed ‘Ghost,’ explores the same design in black. Its dark, translucent bodywork allows glimpses of the mechanical components beneath the surface, including the electrical wiring running through the motorcycle. Without the familiar arrangement of a combustion engine and fuel tank, the design team has been able to reconsider the proportions of a traditional café racer, creating a continuous surface across much of the bike’s body.

Beneath this exterior, the electric powertrain has already demonstrated approximately 100 horsepower, with Real Motors reporting 1,000 Nm of torque at the rear wheel after gearing. The prototype carries an estimated weight of 485 pounds, giving it considerably more mass than its streamlined appearance might suggest. The low handlebars place the rider in the familiar forward-leaning café racer position, with the broad central body occupying much of the space between the knees.

The riding experience is central to the project for Real Motors. The company describes its approach as ‘Electric Analogue,’ an attempt to preserve the physical connection between rider and machine even as the traditional engine gives way to an electric drivetrain. The controls remain familiar, while a small digital display provides essential riding information. The emphasis is on the sensation of operating the motorcycle and the immediate response of its electric motor.

During a recent test ride published by The Verge, journalist Tim Stevens found the Ghost prototype’s acceleration surprisingly smooth, with power delivered progressively across three riding modes. Its handling told a slightly different story. The motorcycle required more deliberate steering through corners than its racing-inspired silhouette might suggest, with the substantial battery weight contributing to its character on the road. The experience offers an early look at how Real Motors’ unconventional design translates into an actual riding machine.

Real Motors is now preparing Lot 01, a limited production run of 250 motorcycles worldwide. The currently listed production configuration uses a 65-kW electric motor paired with a 13.4-kWh battery, with an estimated top speed of 110 mph. These specifications differ from those of the higher-powered prototypes and Founder’s Edition. Prices for Lot 01 begin at $20,000, with North American deliveries currently anticipated in early 2027.

The company is also offering a selection of exterior liveries, allowing customers to choose how the sculptural bodywork will be finished. Its earlier Founder’s Edition is already sold out, while final specifications for Lot 01 are expected to be disclosed before manufacturing begins. The production timeline has shifted since the motorcycle’s initial announcement, but with working prototypes now on the street, Project: ARES is steadily approaching its first customer deliveries.

Tesla Extends Cybertruck Production Run To Work Through Order Backlog

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Browse this and many other websites without banner ads, personalized tracking and video ads.

Tesla will now make the Cybertruck for a little while longer to fulfill more orders, Elon Musk said on Wednesday.

"In order to work through more of the Cybertruck order backlog, production will be extended through the end of summer 2027," he said in an X post, adding in a reply, "This is not the smartest move from a profit-maximizing standpoint btw, but there must be some room in this world for sentimentality or what’s the point?"

In a late-night tweet last week, Musk announced the forthcoming demise of the stainless-steel pickup in the middle of next year to make more room for production of steering wheel-free Cybercabs at Tesla's Texas factory. He later clarified that the truck would be made for eight more months, pointing to an expiration date of July 2027 for what is surely the strangest and most polarizing pickup ever made.

Now production is poised to last through most of September, giving the Cybertruck a roughly 10-month runway. Musk had previously said Tesla would have to return some deposits as it wouldn't be able to deliver all the trucks customers had ordered before production came to a close.

Elon Musk said Tesla is extending Cybertruck production so it can fulfill more orders.

It's still an untimely end for a model that attracted more controversy than cold, hard sales. Musk once targeted an annual output of 250,000 trucks, which would make it a top seller in the pickup market. Tesla ended up selling some 70,000 of them, per industry estimates, since sales kicked off at the end of 2023.

In part, extending the truck's production run may be a way to placate Tesla's die-hard fans, some of whom expressed outrage at the decision to cancel it so suddenly and unceremoniously. Say what you will about the truck itself, but it has amassed a loyal following like few other vehicles have. Over 5,000 people have signed a petition to keep the Cybertruck in production. Unplugged Performance, which makes Tesla accessories, said it's trying to get investors on board to take over Cybertruck production from Tesla.

Now those Tesla devotees have a little more time to buy a Cybertruck. It'll cost them dearly, though. Since announcing the truck's cancelation, Tesla made the top-trim, tri-motor Cyberbeast the only one available—and hiked its price to $110,000.

GTA 7 could finally introduce a completely new city as Rockstar opens door to leaving America

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Rockstar Games has opened the door to taking Grand Theft Auto to a completely new city or even potentially leaving the United States for GTA 7 rather than revisiting familiar locations.

With GTA 6 launching on November 19, players are preparing to return to Vice City, Rockstar’s fictional version of Miami, alongside the wider state of Leonida.

However, the franchise has spent decades revisiting the same American cities, including Liberty City, Los Santos, and Vice City. That has left fans wondering whether a future installment could finally break the cycle.

Now, one of Rockstar’s top developers has suggested that an international setting isn’t entirely off the table.

Speaking to The Guardian, Rockstar North co-studio head Rob Nelson was asked whether the company could ever envision a GTA game set outside the United States.

“Never say never, but there’s still so much to do in America,” Nelson responded.

While Nelson didn’t specifically mention GTA 7 or confirm plans to abandon American cities, his comments leave the possibility open for future installments. Even if the series continues taking place in the United States, it also sounds like we could finally see Grand Theft Auto tackle a fresh location.

Interestingly, Rockstar has already explored taking GTA overseas.

As Dexerto previously reported, former Rockstar North technical director Obbe Vermeij revealed that GTA: Tokyo was once seriously considered, with a Japanese developer potentially taking charge of the project.

Other international locations discussed included Rio de Janeiro, Moscow, Istanbul, London, and Paris. However, none of those concepts progressed into a completed game.

Vermeij was considerably less optimistic about the franchise leaving America when he discussed GTA’s future in January 2026.

Ex-GTA dev says Rockstar are “stuck” using same cities in-game as others aren’t “realistic”

Former Rockstar director reveals new details behind scrapped GTA: Tokyo project

Ex-Rockstar dev reveals GTA: Tokyo was planned alongside other countries

He argued that Rockstar was effectively stuck revisiting American cities because their familiarity and cultural influence made them safer choices for games with enormous budgets.

Nelson’s latest comments suggest the company hasn’t completely closed the door on exploring somewhere different.

For now, Rockstar’s focus remains firmly on GTA 6, which has taken more than a decade to develop and features the series’ most detailed recreation of America yet.

And while a former Rockstar developer believes GTA 7 could already be in development, the studio has yet to officially announce the next installment or reveal where it might take place.

TikTok Shop testing $10 Amazon Prime-style subscription service for shoppers

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

TikTok Shop is testing a new paid subscription service that offers shoppers exclusive discounts, free shipping, and other benefits similar to Amazon Prime.

The new membership, called TikTok Shop Plus, is being introduced to select customers in the US as the social media platform prepares for the holiday shopping season.

TikTok Shop launched in the US in 2023 and has since expanded its presence in online retail. The subscription pilot comes as the company looks to attract more shoppers and compete with established retailers like Amazon and Walmart.

TikTok Shop began offering its new membership program over the weekend to more than 1 million US customers identified as some of its most loyal shoppers.

The service costs $9.99 per month following a 30-day free trial, making it cheaper than Amazon Prime’s $14.99 monthly subscription and Walmart+’s $12.95 monthly membership.

Members also receive three coupons each month, including $5 off with no minimum purchase, $10 off orders of $59 or more, and $30 off purchases totaling at least $119.

“TikTok Shop Plus is currently being piloted with an expanding group of eligible U.S. users,” Patrick Nommensen, president of strategic initiatives at TikTok Shop, told Modern Retail.

TikTok reportedly eyeing ad-free viewing experience with new premium subscription

TikTok Shop to rival Amazon with early Black Friday deals

TikTok launches LIVE subscriptions to compete with Twitch and YouTube Gaming

“As we gradually scale access to the pilot, we’ll continue to gather feedback and refine the experience, with the goal of delivering more value and convenience to shoppers.”

The company previously tested a version of the subscription program in July 2026, according to Business Insider. The latest pilot expands access to more than 1 million eligible customers, although TikTok has not announced when the membership might become available to everyone.

The shop is expanding its retail offerings ahead of the holidays, with major companies including Best Buy, Ulta Beauty, and Sephora participating in the platform’s shopping experience for the first time this holiday season.

The company has also introduced an AI-powered shopping assistant to help customers discover products and allows purchases directly through the For You feed without leaving the app.

For now, the new subscription remains an invitation-only pilot, with the company continuing to collect customer feedback before determining how broadly to expand the service.

New Sensor Tech Could Replace Bayer Color Filter and Transform Mobile Photography

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

New Sensor Tech Could Replace Bayer Color Filter and Transform Mobile Photography

Metalenz has acquired Israeli startup PxE Imaging. PxE’s claim to fame is its Holocoder technology, a replacement for the Bayer color filter found on traditional image sensors.

As Metalenz explains in its press release, the Holocoder “eliminates the need for the Bayer filter and sorts light without absorption, increasing the light delivered to a standard image sensor by 2.5x.”

As expected, significantly increasing the light delivered to an image sensor will, all else equal, dramatically improve camera performance and image quality across the board—in dark scenes and bright ones alike.

Like PxE Imaging, Metalenz has been focused on technologies that adhere directly to an image sensor. A few years ago, Metalenz brought its groundbreaking metasurface optics to market, which are ultra-thin optics that can be printed like semiconductor wafers. The key is how the thin lens bends light.

That’s true, too, of Holocoder technology. The Holocoder “harnesses the wave nature of light to physically decode information such as wavelength and depth, enabling simultaneous capture of color and depth information in the same image with a single camera.”

A Bayer filter, which is how traditional digital image sensors capture and reconstruct color, also absorbs about 60% of the light coming into a sensor, Metalenz says. With its acquisition of PxE, Metalenz can now add sophisticated color and depth information to the material and surface data its products already capture, all without significantly increasing manufacturing complexity or computational demands.

“Metalenz has strategically expanded its IP from unique hardware to full-stack solutions co-designed with our metasurfaces,” says Rob Devlin, Co-founder and CEO of Metalenz.

“We’ve deployed into the most demanding customer devices, built a supply chain at mass-market scale, and built a software stack that takes information delivered to a sensor all the way through AI decisions. With PxE’s technology and team, we’re expanding our capabilities into visible sensing, addressing a longstanding problem of low light performance while solving the new physical AI problem of obtaining richer information beyond conventional RGB imagery,” Devlin continues.

“Owning hardware that brings new information to sensors is the real moat for differentiated AI models. Our combined capability with PxE extends that same principle across all of machine vision, so people and machines can see and understand more about the world without relying on heavy computation.”

“We developed the Holocoder to capture information in light that conventional sensors discard or cannot see, giving cameras greater light sensitivity and the ability to capture color and depth together,” adds Yoav Berlatzky, Co-founder and CEO of PxE Imaging.

“Joining Metalenz gives us the manufacturing platform and commercial reach to bring this technology to everyday devices. Together, we can give people better images and machines a richer understanding of the world.”

The terms of the transaction have not been disclosed.

Devlin spoke to WIRED about the acquisition, shedding more light, pun intended, on PxE Imaging’s technology.

“Apple, Samsung, Google — everyone is fighting to improve the visible camera every year, right?” Devlin tells WIRED.

“A lot of what they’re doing is investing hundreds of millions of dollars into software to try to somehow improve it, but the downside of that is that once information is lost when you’re absorbing all of that light, you can’t really buy it back, even if you’re throwing all of the software into the world at it.”

As of right now, Metalenz cannot directly combine PxE’s Holocoder technology with its commercial Metalenz optics. However, it is absolutely in the cards for the long term. PxE’s technology itself could hit the market as soon as the end of next year, thanks to Metalenz’s manufacturing capacity.

The potential use cases here are manifold. In scenarios where tiny cameras are very important, like autonomous vehicles for example, it’s obvious that a smaller camera that can absorb more light is beneficial.

Another obvious situation is smartphone cameras. As Devlin notes, there is a physical problem that image sensors of all sizes must try to overcome: getting more, better light data. The more light that reaches the sensor, the better. The Bayer color filter is an incredible technological achievement, but it’s getting very long in the tooth. Perhaps, if PxE is correct, there’s a better way.

Lionel Charbonnier, campione del mondo 1998: "Maignan? Ricorda Barthez: è fortissimo. Ecco cosa gli chiede Fabien in nazionale”

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Parliamo di Mike Maignan con un grande esperto e campione del mondo del 1998: Lionel Charbonnier. Sposato con un’italiana, Charbonnier fu il terzo portiere della Francia nel ’98 e vinse due Coppe di Francia (1994 e 1996) e lo scudetto con il grande AJ Auxerre di Guy Roux, Laurent Blanc, Guivarc’h, Laslandes, Lamouchi e Diomède nel 1996. Un personaggio positivo, schietto e grande conoscitore del calcio, in particolare del ruolo del portiere. Lionel corse ad abbracciare un Fabien Barthez incredulo a terra e in lacrime dopo il fischio finale di Francia-Brasile, la finale della Coppa del Mondo del 1998. Ecco a voi l’analisi di Lionel Charbonnier, consulente per RMC e L’After Foot. In esclusiva per MilanNews, di Alessandro Schiavone.

«È un buon portiere e gli voglio molto bene. In passato ha avuto un piccolo passaggio a vuoto, lo so. All’inizio vi aveva tutti sbalorditi quando è arrivato al Milan. Del resto, tutti si chiedevano perché. E mio suocero, che è italiano, mi diceva: “Ma siete pazzi, voi francesi, ad aver preso Gigio al posto di Maignan! E com’è possibile che il PSG non abbia preso Maignan?”. Effettivamente, all’inizio da voi era eccellente. Poi ha avuto un piccolo passaggio a vuoto. Il suo gioco è cambiato e penso che oggi sia tornato quello che era! Penso che ci sia stato un fenomeno di adattamento e poi un gioco diverso. Ma con la nazionale francese l’ho ritrovato con Fabien Barthez, che gli ha chiesto di partecipare maggiormente al gioco, di giocare di più con i piedi, di stare un po’ più alto e di essere più naturale. Mentre al Milan l’ho trovato un po’ più chiuso nei suoi sei metri, e questo lo ha penalizzato. Doveva acquisire nuovi punti di riferimento e penso che frequentare Fabien gli farà davvero, davvero molto bene per il finale della sua carriera».

Agilità e flessibilità: ha ereditato qualcosa da Barthez?

«È un po’ la stessa predisposizione, è un po’ questa destrezza. Innanzitutto, è molto agile, è un portiere un po’ volante. Inoltre, non è maldestro con i piedi. Non è certo Zizou, ma insomma, sa parare i palloni, ed è quello che si chiede a un portiere. Ma acquisendo fiducia in questo modo con i piedi nel calcio moderno, evolverà molto. Bisogna però dargli molta più libertà rispetto alla sua porta, perché… Se è troppo sotto pressione, troppo vicino alla sua porta, può inciampare. Ma…»

«Per il resto, sì, ha questa qualità sulla linea, ha questa grandissima apertura alare. Non lo conosco, ne ho appena parlato con Fabien, ma penso che debba comandare un po’ di più la sua difesa».

C’è un modo diverso di giocare tra Francia e Italia?

«Spesso c’è una differenza culturale e un gioco diverso… La risposta deve trovarla il portiere il più rapidamente possibile! È lui che deve adattarsi nello spogliatoio, nel gioco e a tutto il resto! Mi ricordo che quando ero all’Auxerre, Guy Roux vietava i retropassaggi e soprattutto mi vietava di uscire dai sei metri sui palloni alti. Quindi, a volte, questo alleggerisce la pressione sulla difesa, ma persino quando prendevo il pallone, lui mi sgridava! Ma quando sono arrivato in nazionale francese mi dicevano: “No, no, lì devi andare dentro l’area dei sedici metri!”. Avevo una partita per adattarmi e ho dovuto farlo. Quindi ho avuto quella partita per riuscirci con la nazionale francese. Successivamente sono andato ai Glasgow Rangers e lì mi hanno detto che dovevo giocare da libero. Vale a dire che il giocatore che era il numero 10 si girava verso di me e mi passava la palla. Ma da noi questa cosa non esisteva: il numero 10 è quello che serve gli attaccanti. Ho dovuto adattarmi subito. Ho avuto una o due partite, ma dopo non si ha più il diritto di avere passaggi a vuoto. Con Mike non so cosa sia successo nella sua testa, ma è vero che, secondo me, ci ha messo un po’ di tempo… E credo che al Milan sia stato persino un po’ messo in discussione».

Cosa ne pensa di Donnarumma? A proposito di Francia-Italia…

«Dollarumma, è così?» (Ride, ndr). Gigio è cresciuto moltissimo, moltissimo. Gigio, rispetto a Mike… Mike era capace di essere determinante già alla sua prima parata. Gigio, all’inizio, non lo era quando è arrivato al PSG. L’ha pagato, perché aveva bisogno di fare due o tre parate, o addirittura di subire uno o due gol, per essere performante! Capitava spesso, agli inizi della prima esperienza di Gigio al PSG dopo il suo arrivo a Parigi, che il PSG non avesse il pallone. Di conseguenza, Gigio veniva bombardato di tiri e, dopo il secondo gol subito, diventava molto bravo e ci si diceva: “Tornerà quello di prima!”. Ma no: quando sei al PSG, devi essere molto forte già alla prima parata. Ha avuto difficoltà a superare questo ostacolo, ma penso che fosse seguito male. Vale a dire che, quando si hanno portieri del genere, l’allenatore dei portieri è molto importante. E Gigio è cresciuto con il PSG. A un certo punto se l’è messo in testa, ha capito che la sua prima parata era determinante, perché gli avversari, subito dopo, avrebbero segnato. Quindi, invece di perdere 1-0, il PSG vinceva 1-0. E se il PSG vince 1-0, cambia tutto. Ed è lì che Gigio ha fatto il salto di qualità. Per Maignan potrebbe essere la stessa cosa, ma al contrario. Vale a dire che, quando è arrivato, era determinante alla prima parata. Ma poi è tornato a essere quello che Gigio era al PSG. Subiva uno o due gol e poi, ogni tanto, faceva una parata. Penso onestamente che l’ambiente che circonda un portiere sia molto, molto importante! Bisogna avere un allenatore dei portieri che sia un consigliere, perché a questi giocatori non si insegna più soltanto a giocare, ma anche come affrontare psicologicamente la prima parata, come prepararsi all’inizio della partita e tutto il resto… “Attenzione, ti succederà questo, questo e questo”. Soltanto coloro che hanno conosciuto il livello più alto, quello davvero altissimo, possono aiutarti. Possiamo anche essere ottimi allenatori, ma se l’allenatore dei portieri non ha giocato, il suo discorso, anche se ha ragione, non arriva allo stesso modo. Se invece si tratta di un campione del mondo o di uno che ha avuto una grande carriera da portiere, allora veniamo subito ascoltati. Il giocatore impara molto di più e acquisisce fiducia. Può dirgli: “Io l’ho vissuto in un determinato momento, ho fatto questo, ho fatto quello… e tutto è andato bene”. E a quel punto il discorso arriva, anche se si usano le stesse parole, ma pronunciate da una persona diversa».

Exterminauts looks like another Helldivers 2, but it's really the Deep Rock Galactic sickos who should take notice

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The Dead By Daylight studio takes on a co-op shooter.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

It's best to go into game demos with a clear mind, but that was never in the cards for Exterminauts, the new co-op game from Dead by Daylight studio Behavior Interactive. Everything about Exterminauts, externally, screams Helldivers 2, from its working class soldiers and anonymized outfits to its hostile wildlife and colorfully pulpy planets.

Exterminauts' developers, who are a distinct team from the one making Dead By Daylight, see the comparisons coming and don't shy away from them, but they also point out that their game was in development for years before Arrowhead's hit shooter came out in 2024.

They also draw a distinction in tone: Exterminauts does not cast you as a dedicated servant of intergalactic democracy, but as militarized, blue-collar pest control cleansing the planet of Tian so that corporate interests can better sap the land of its resources. In that way and more, Exterminauts has more in common with Deep Rock Galactic or Mycopunk.

That's a realization that helped me acclimate to Exterminauts after a few hours with an alpha build—it's got Deep Rock-like milestones, class roles, and mission types. Loadouts begin with a choice of a soft class (called sponsors) that grants passive bonuses and a unique power weapon. There's one that comes with a medic tool and a damage-focused lightning gun that I gravitated toward. You can also bring along gadgets like ammo boxes, med packs, and automated turrets that could singlehandedly flatten a horde (Behaviour admitted these were overpowered at the moment).

It's relevant to note that my developer-led demo stayed on the lower difficulties, but even with that in mind, Exterminauts struck me as more casual than games like it. Guns are very easy to control with a mouse, friendly fire is either barely there or nonexistent, ammo was readily available through pickups at every point of interest, and there's a generous respawn mechanic that lets you summon teammates back into the action.

The maps I played were straightforward canyons littered with corporate mining facilities. Objectives were clearly marked on the map from the start, as are side tasks. There's clear Helldivers inspiration in the jobs themselves—there's a lot of solving micro puzzles on computer screens to get a facility back online—but I never really felt rushed to complete them because the fauna barely put up a fight.

I'd like to see what Exterminauts is like when it's challenging, because its creative creatures have a lot more bark than they do bite. No bugs or robot hordes here—Behaviour is leaning into freaky plant monsters and flying squids. I especially enjoyed the giant carnivorous flytraps that crawl on six feet and the flying, spiky tulips that slow down enemies until they're dealt with.

Keep up to date with the most important stories and the best deals, as picked by the PC Gamer team.

Cranking up the difficulty might also bring the depth of its classes into focus—each sponsor (class) can be modified through firmware unlocked through play. These looked like small bonuses, like extra freeze damage or HP gained on kill, that can be stacked to make a bigger impact.

Behaviour is planning large and frequent updates for Exterminauts, including 12-week seasons and a constant drip feed of sponsorship packages that introduce new weapons, gadgets, cosmetics, and firmware modifiers. The idea is to make these relatively easy to unlock through normal play, but you'll be able to buy them early for real money, too.

A general lack of energy led to my demo feeling rudimentary and sometimes boring—I was craving a sense of risk or struggle that gives co-op shooters moment-to-moment stakes, but never got it. These strike me as problems that can be solved, and Exterminauts does have good bones, so I'll be interested to try it again when it's more complete.

Some Of These Harsh Reviews Of How To Rob A Bank Are Giving Me 2025's War Of The Worlds Vibes

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Join The Club for quick access. Enter your email below and we'll send confirmation, and sign you up to our newsletter.

Godzilla Minus Zero Reviews Have Me Rethinking My Weekend Plans

I Watched Ali G: Who Iz I? And I Genuinely Don't Know How I Feel. Here's Why

One Big Thing That's Keeping Me From Thinking Wild Horse Nine Is One Of This Year's Best

Unlock instant access to exclusive member features.

Get full access to premium articles, exclusive features and a growing list of member rewards.

A muddled political message hidden behind a TikTok-style format

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Reviews have dropped for How to Rob A Bank, starring Nicolaus Holt, Zoë Kravitz, Pete Davidson, Anna Sawai, and John C. Reilly, and they aren’t pretty. The movie, directed by David Leitch (Deadpool 2, The Fall Guy), hits the 2026 movie schedule this week. The reviews are not as uniformly negative as they were for another Amazon MGM movie, 2025’s War of the Worlds (How to Rob a Bank is hovering just south of 50% on Rotten Tomatoes), but they are giving me the same vibes. That’s not a good thing.

Even in some of the more positive reviews, writers aren’t impressed by the heist movie’s seemingly ham-fisted attempt to bring the classic heist movie genre into the 2020s in its presentation (and political message), just like War of the Worlds attempted (poorly) to update that classic sci-fi story with Amazon drones and Ice Cube responding to text messages for an entire movie. Caroline Siede, writing for Decoding Everything, says:

How to Rob a Bank wants to be a rallying cry for social revolution via internet community building, but it winds up feeling more like a mindless TikTok scroll.

Even the more positive reviews, like Cortlyn Kelly's 2.5-star review for Roger Ebert, call attention to this:

Breaking the silver-screen barrier, the ringleader, Ryan (Hoult), addresses his audience by stating how important they are; almost like a double entendre, the movie speaks to both its fictional audience and those in the theater. This focus on the audience is intriguing, adding depth to an otherwise standard money-heist flick. Pandering to a younger generation by contentifying the plot makes it easy for ‘How to Rob a Bank’ to one day be screened as a 212-part TikTok series.

The same can be said for the positive-ish review from The Hollywood Reporter, which is pretty harsh on the social media aspects of the film. Writer David Rooney says:

While the robberies are diverting enough and the YouTube inserts splashed with pop-up graphics, the fun wears a bit thin, which makes the shift into trauma and pathos a welcome one, even if the transition might have been smoother.

The Globe and Mail’s review of How to Rob a Bank also questions the presentation of the movie in social media form, with film critic Radheyan Simonpillai writing:

This is not a heist movie. It’s the online hack to a heist movie, curiously made for the big screen, seeking the path of least resistance to constant dopamine hits. We’re foregoing all the labour of a heist movie because it would likely be oh so tedious for the movie’s target audience, presumably the generation under 30, whose work ethic is constantly called into question.

The fact that How to Rob A Bank also tried to make an anti-establishment, anti-banking movie while being financed by Amazon isn’t helping its case, per some reviews.

For example, in his 2-star review for The Guardian, Benjamin Lee wrote that not only does the movie feel like it was put together by someone trying to nail the “right now” of social media and missing badly, but the political message is also lost:

It’s a little hard to stomach an anti-capitalism screed not only funded by Amazon but featuring several scenes of lame, please-aim-logo-towards-camera, product placement (after one dismantles the system, a refreshing can of Mountain Dew really does hit the spot).

Writing for The Daily Beast, Nick Schager puts the film’s attempt at a political statement even more succinctly:

Their film attempts to rage against the machine from within the (Amazon/MGM) machine.

In fact, Amazon and its CEO, Jeff Bezos, find themselves at the heart of a lot of the negative reviews. William Bibbiani at The Wrap writes:

It’s an Amazon-MGM production, which makes the film’s efforts to sell anti-capitalism as entertainment hypocritical, laughable and insulting. It’s a film that says you should stand up to Jeff Bezos, but only after you pay Jeff Bezos. No matter how cool “Atomic Blonde” director David Leitch tries to make bank robbing look, this is the opposite of cool.

I would like to note that not all of the reviews are scathing, but none are overwhelmingly positive, either. The movie seems like it delivers on action for many reviews (though some didn’t like it), but the great action sequences don’t seem to save it from itself, even if some reviews found How to Rob a Bank fun enough.

The movie also doesn’t look like it’ll be all that big of a hit at the box office, meaning it’ll likely be available to watch with a Prime subscription pretty quickly. We’ll have to see on that.

Eikichi Iwai, Bassist of Final Fantasy XIV Band THE PRIMALS, Has Passed Away

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Michael Manning (He/Him) has been enamored with video games since he first played the Sonic Classic Collection as a child. With decades of experience in gaming and a full tour as a Gaming Journalist, Michael is ready to step up to the plate. From AAA to the smallest passion projects, his motto is that every piece of ar

Music makes many games what they truly are, setting the tone and doubling down on a moment's emotions, whether somber, pulse-pounding, or chilling. Without their OSTs, many games would feel like a shell of themselves, solid without the soul. Many games have used the need for music to get musicians on the map or back into the public spotlight, creating a beautiful loop.

The EU's proposed Digital Fairness Act targets loot boxes, pay-to-win mechanics, unclear in-game prices, and subscription traps.

Final Fantasy is a series absolutely defined by its music, with truly legendary composers and artists stemming from every title. Sadly, Final Fantasy 14’s Twitter announced earlier today that Eikichi Iwai, bassist for the band made for the game THE PRIMALS, has passed away. After recently performing at the most recent Japanese Fan Fest, his passing is a sudden low blow that has staggered fans across the globe.

THE PRIMALS are the standout musicians of Final Fantasy 14, far from the only incredibly talented maestros making the music happen, but definitely the flashiest. Often brought out to live events for both their own original tracks and covers of some of 14's other greatest hits, THE PRIMALS helped forge some of the greatest tracks to grace Final Fantasy 14, and Iwai-san's absence will be deeply felt in future events.

While the rest of the musicians behind FF 14 and his bandmates on THE PRIMALS will feel the most potent loss, fans across the globe are aching as well, already holding in-game mourning vigils throughout Eorzea. Sitting by fires and playing different pieces of music Iwai worked on, fans are lamenting his passing the best way they know how, by celebrating all he gave to them.

THE PRIMALS are the standout musicians of Final Fantasy 14, far from the only incredibly talented maestros making the music happen, but definitely the flashiest.

THE PRIMALS have backed some of the most pivotal moments in Final Fantasy 14’s history, bouncing from raid layers to incredibly crucial story moments to general world traversal. As a band, they’re incredibly flexible and talented, with a good part of that grit coming from Iwai’s incredible bass play. The future of the band is currently uncertain, especially with how sudden Iwai’s loss truly was.

Eikichi Iwai passed at age 57, a titan of his craft and an incredible musician across his pursuits, charming millions who played Final Fantasy 14 and all who followed his long history with the band Chirinuruwowaka, of which he was a founding member. Never one to slow down or stop making music, with his recent showing at the latest Japanese FF14 Fan Fest being cited as one of THE PRIMALS best ever showings.

After suffering a loss like Iwai’s, it’s important to remember just how lucky we are to enjoy such incredibly skilled artists in their primes. Final Fantasy 14 has its issues, but music has never been one of them, carrying many crucial emotional scenes with next-level tracks again and again.

Close in the Distance, A Long Fall, and Rise, all written by Masayoshi Soken, show a mastery of tone and emotion across an incredible range, perfectly putting players in the mindset of the current moment. THE PRIMALS’ own coverage of tracks like Footfalls and Dawntrail gives each song life, fueled by THE PRIMALS’ incredible energy.

I genuinely believe you could find someone who cites almost every song in Final Fantasy 14 as one of their favorites, and that’s amazing. The incredible display of artistry throughout the title is a large reason why it has lasted as long as it has with the massive fanbase it has cultivated.

With live service games and MMOs like Final Fantasy 14, it can be all too easy and lucrative to fall into the trappings of consumerism, focusing on the product rather than the art. FF really doesn’t have that issue. FF14 has always felt like a title guided by the artists who create it, and after the loss of such a powerful artist, it’s up to the rest of us to keep on that path. Rest in peace, Eikichi Iwai.

Deutsche Bank Goes Live With Crypto Custody After a Five-Year Buildup

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Deutsche Bank Goes Live With Crypto Custody After a Five-Year Buildup

Five years after it first floated the idea, Germany’s largest lender is now holding Bitcoin for clients.

Deutsche Bank said Wednesday that its Digital Asset Custody service has gone live for paying clients, giving institutional and corporate customers in Europe a regulated way to store and transfer Bitcoin, Ethereum and three stablecoins: Circle’s USDC and EURC, plus EURAU.

The bank manages wallets and private keys on clients’ behalf. For now, the service covers custody and transfers, with tokenized financial instruments on its roadmap.

The service is aimed at clients of Deutsche Bank’s corporate and investment banking units, including corporations, asset managers, hedge funds, custodians, brokers and sovereign institutions, according to the bank’s September announcement of the plan.

Its setup includes hardware-based key protection, multi-person approvals and separate warm and cold storage, the bank said.

“In the medium term we aim to deliver a fully integrated, regulated ecosystem that supports the custody, tokenisation, and settlement of digital assets,” said Paul Maley, Deutsche Bank’s global head of trust and securities services.

Deutsche Bank also signed a memorandum of understanding with Deutsche Asset Management, the majority-owned money manager formerly known as DWS, to explore working together on tokenized money market funds, crypto exchange-traded commodities and stablecoins.

The asset manager oversaw about 1.22 trillion euros as of Sept. 30. The bank has also said it plans to expand the custody service to Asia.

The launch caps a long road, going back to at least 2021 when Deutsche Bank began readying a crypto custody and trading platform. Then, in 2023, the bank applied for a digital asset custody license in Germany. Last year, reports said the bank planned to build the service with technology from crypto exchange Bitpanda, alongside its longtime partner Taurus.

Deutsche Bank received authorization from German regulator BaFin under the EU’s Markets in Crypto-Assets regulation, or MiCA, which requires crypto service providers to be licensed. The bank cautioned that its custody service doesn’t protect clients against swings in the value of digital assets, fraud or failures by other market participants.

The custody launch isn’t Deutsche Bank’s only crypto bet. The bank is among a group of global lenders, including Goldman Sachs and Citi, backing a $270 million challenge to crypto’s dominant stablecoin issuers.

Why Is Crypto Crashing? Elon Musk’s Grok AI Predicts BTC to $40K

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Ahmed Barakat is a journalist and copywriter based in Georgia with a growing focus on blockchain technology, DeFi, AI, privacy, digital assets, and fintech innovation.

The CryptoNews editorial team is composed of seasoned writers specializing in cryptocurrency and blockchain technology. Their expertise ensures comprehensive, accurate, and insightful content for...

Why is crypto crashing today? BTC/USD fell to $73,700 overnight, down another 0.8%, as $744M in liquidations triggered another market-wide crash after hotter-than-expected CPI data on November 10 spiked fears of further rate hikes later this year. With macroeconomic tailwinds driving high volatility, Elon Musk’s Grok AI predicts Bitcoin could crash to $40K by the end of 2026.

Bitcoin is currently trading in the mid-$73,000s range. However, a highly unusual and severe downside scenario could develop before 2027. In this extreme bearish outlook, a significant catalyst, though very unlikely, might trigger a rapid and sustained decline in market confidence and liquidity. This could lead to a dramatic drop in Bitcoin’s price by January 1, 2027.

Technically, Bitcoin has already shown signs of distribution after failing to sustain higher levels earlier in the cycle. Price has been making lower highs on the weekly chart and is currently trading below several key moving averages that previously acted as support.

A decisive break below the recent swing lows near $70,000–$72,000 would open the door to a deeper measured-move decline targeting the mid-$50,000s, with further downside extensions possible if sell-side volume expands.

Momentum indicators such as the RSI remain subdued on higher timeframes, lacking the strong positive divergence typically needed for a durable recovery. In a true capitulation scenario driven by the catalyst above, classic support zones around $50,000 and then $45,000 could give way relatively quickly as leveraged positions unwind and long-term holders finally capitulate.

While such an extreme outcome remains highly improbable under normal market and regulatory conditions, the combination of forced institutional selling and a shattered confidence narrative could theoretically produce one of the sharper multi-month drawdowns in Bitcoin’s history.

🚨 FED RATE HIKE IS ALMOST CONFIRMED NOWUS inflation data was released, and it again came in higher than expectations.US CPI rose 0.4% MoM, while the Core CPI rose 0.3% MoM.Last week, the job data already came better than expected, which means the Fed's biggest concern is… pic.twitter.com/vgTAVctnAR

Why is crypto crashing today? Put simply, cryptocurrency markets are under pressure largely because of stronger-than-expected U.S. inflation data. The Consumer Price Index (CPI) for October rose 0.4% month-over-month, exceeding the forecast of 0.3%.

Meanwhile, core CPI rose 0.3%, above expectations of 0.2%. This unexpected rise in inflation has increased speculation that the Federal Reserve may raise interest rates in December.

This, in turn, has pushed Treasury yields higher and dampened interest in riskier assets. As a result, Bitcoin has crashed to around $73,500-$73,800, and Ethereum and several major altcoins have also declined significantly over the past 48 hours.

Adding to the selling pressure are ongoing outflows from U.S. spot Bitcoin and Ethereum ETFs, as well as significant liquidations of leveraged long positions amounting to hundreds of millions, per CoinGlass data.

The combination of anticipated tighter monetary policy and reduced institutional demand has contributed to the prolonged downturn in the cryptocurrency market.

The hypothetical catalyst centers on a coordinated regulatory and institutional shock. Imagine several major economies, including the United States and key G7 partners, simultaneously classifying Bitcoin as a systemic financial risk following a series of high-profile custody failures or unexpected volatility events.

New emergency rules force banks, pension funds, and regulated investment vehicles to reduce or eliminate Bitcoin exposure within months. Spot ETF outflows accelerate into a self-reinforcing spiral, while large corporate treasury holders and early institutional adopters begin liquidating positions to comply.

The “digital gold” narrative fractures under forced selling and negative headlines, creating a prolonged risk-off environment that few current holders are prepared for. As a result, Grok AI predicts the Bitcoin price will fall to approximately $40,000 by January 1, 2027.

Explore: The Hottest Presale Plays in November

While investors are asking ‘Why is crypto crashing today?’ and Elon’s Grok AI predicts BTC to $40K, Bitcoin Hyper (HYPER) is stepping in as a safe-haven, early-stage play focused on another major opportunity: bringing high-speed financial applications to the Bitcoin ecosystem.

As the largest cryptocurrency by market capitalization, Bitcoin attracts a wide range of holders and investors. Bitcoin Hyper aims to leverage this liquidity by using a Layer 2 network designed to facilitate faster transactions and support decentralized finance (DeFi) functionalities.

The platform runs on the Solana Virtual Machine (SVM), bringing Solana-style execution capabilities to the Bitcoin-focused ecosystem.

Bitcoin Hyper aims to combine Bitcoin’s significant liquidity and established investor base with transaction speeds that meet the needs of modern decentralized applications. Key features include near-instant transaction finality and low costs, which are essential to its market positioning.

Bitcoin Hyper has drawn significant attention through its presale, raising over $33.2M and showing strong early fundraising momentum. It offers a 35% APY in staking rewards, incentivizing early participants. Token holders can stake their HYPER tokens to earn additional rewards instead of leaving their allocations idle.

With the planned November 26 launch date approaching, the presale is entering a critical phase. Those interested in Bitcoin’s expanding Layer 2 ecosystem are eagerly anticipating Hyper’s market debut before it lists on centralized exchanges and euphoric demand takes control of the chart.

Bank Of America’s No Annual Fee Card Pays 6% In Year One — Here’s The Best Way To Use It

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The Bank of America® Customized Cash Rewards credit card pays 6% back in a category you choose during your first year, with no annual fee. That can include online shopping, phone and internet bills, or home repairs. Bank of America® Customized Cash Rewards‘ initial bonus offer is a $200 online cash rewards bonus after you make at least $1,000 in purchases in the first 90 days of account opening. I like finding worthwhile cards outside the familiar bank discussions. More competition is good, and a card can deserve some of your spending without replacing everything else in your wallet. What The Card Currently Offers The standard consumer card has: No annual fee. A $200 initial bonus after $1,000 in purchases within 90 days of account opening. 6% in your selected category during the first year: the normal 3% plus a temporary extra 3%. 2% at grocery stores and wholesale clubs. 1% on other purchases. The higher rates all share a $2,500 quarterly spending allowance; once you’ve used it, those purchases earn 1% too. Banking relationship bonuses can increase earning. For the choice earning category, you can select among gas and electric vehicle charging; online shopping, including cable, internet, phone plans and streaming; dining; travel; drug stores and pharmacies; or home improvement and furnishings. You can change your category once each calendar month for future purchases. Leave it alone and your selection carries forward. It starts out as gas and charging, so someone getting the card for online shopping needs to change that setting. Earning scales withBofA RewardsTM based on qualifying funds with the bank: Relationship Tier And Qualifying Balance Choice Category, First Year Choice Category, Afterward Groceries And Wholesale Clubs Other Spending / After Cap Not enrolled 6% 3% 2% 1% Member: no minimum balance 6.3% 3.3% 2.2% 1.1% Preferred Plus: $30,000+ 6.75% 3.75% 2.5% 1.25% Preferred Honors: $100,000+ 7.5% 4.5% 3% 1.5% Premier: $1 million+ 8.25% 5.25% 3.5% 1.75% The first three rewards columns remain subject to the shared quarterly spending limit. The banking program requires enrollment and an eligible personal checking account. Higher tiers generally use your combined qualifying average balances over three months. The relationship bonuses are 10%, 25%, 50% and 75%. They apply to the normal earnings, while the extra 3% first-year bonus isn’t multiplied. (At the highest tier the calculation is 3% × 1.75 + 3% = 8.25%, not 10.5%). Qualifying Merrill investments, including eligible retirement accounts, count toward the balance requirement. You don’t have to leave $100,000 sitting in a checking account. Online Shopping And Home Improvement Are My Preferred Categories For someone who already has good travel cards, I’d look first at purchases those cards don’t bonus. Online shopping would be my default selection. Bank of America’s category definitions include purchases through websites and apps, plus cable, internet, phone plans and streaming. Merchant examples include Amazon, Walmart, Etsy and Ticketmaster. That covers purchases across different merchants with one selection, and you can combine eligible shopping portal rewards with the cash back from paying by card. Online shopping has exclusions: taxes, insurance, medical bills, tuition, utilities and various non-retail services don’t become bonus purchases by paying on the website. Home improvement and furnishings. The bank includes qualifying contractors such as plumbers and electricians, alongside furniture and hardware stores. I’d keep online shopping selected most of the time and switch before paying for a repair or furniture purchase. Travel is useful, especially in the first year. The bank includes airlines and hotels, but also cruises, vacation rentals, attractions, transit and parking. You can book directly rather than going through a bank’s travel portal. That makes it worth considering for a domestic vacation rental or cruise payment. I earn well with other cards on airfare and hotels, but bonusing short-term rentals and cruises can fill a gap. How I’d Fit It Into A Travel Rewards Wallet I’d rate this 4 out of 5 as a spending category specialist for someone who can use the first-year offer or who qualifies for elevated banking rewards. It’s best for someone with online purchases or home improvement bills that otherwise earn an ordinary return. The standard card charges a 3% foreign transaction fee. This is for domestic purchases. And even the highest relationship tier pays just 1.75% on ordinary spending and after the cap, less than a straightforward 2% card. You can redeem cash back for statement credits without a minimum, or deposit it into eligible Bank of America or Merrill accounts. Checks, qualifying Merrill 529 contributions and automatic redemptions have $25 minimums. Rewards don’t expire while the account remains open. I’d take the cash. It can pay award ticket taxes, a hotel outside a chain, or anything else. Andy Weir's 'Exoplanet' just landed on Audible, but what actually makes an exoplanet habitable?

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

How would the crew of the Wild Blue Yonder fare on a real exoplanet?

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Imagine you’ve crash-landed on an alien planet. Your ship is destroyed, your oxygen supply is dangerously low, and the landscape around you is completely unknown. Where would you begin looking for clues to your survival? Surprisingly, the best place to start could be the star overhead.

This is the predicament that three crew members of the Wild Blue Yonder face in Audible’s new sci-fi thriller, "Exoplanet." This sci-fi thriller is written by acclaimed comic writer Benjamin Percy, executive produced by Andy Weir (Project Hail Mary, The Martian), and features an all-star voice cast including Anthony Mackie, Ariana DeBose, and John Leguizamo.

Stranded on an unfamiliar planet, they encounter volcanic wastelands, unknown skies, and environments that seem to defy reason. But behind the extraordinary events of this audio drama is a very real scientific question: what makes a planet habitable?

In “Exoplanet”, the crew view skies that are not only unfamiliar but also seemingly different from one another. One character sees a sun as so dim that he can look directly at it, while another suffers under a blazing heat, surrounded by stretches of sun-scorched red rock.

For astronomers, the light emitted by a star is one of the most crucial tools for understanding planets orbiting it.

Using a technique called spectroscopy, astronomers split starlight into its constituent wavelengths, producing a spectrum much like a rainbow. By studying this spectrum, they can investigate the temperature, composition, and other physical properties of the star.

However, stellar temperature is only part of the picture. The amount of energy a planet receives depends on both the luminosity of the star and its orbital distance.

Astronomers use these measurements to determine whether a world lies in the habitable zone of a star, where temperatures could allow liquid water to exist on the surface given a suitable atmosphere.

A planet orbiting a cool red dwarf would need to be much closer to its star than Earth is to the Sun to be in this habitable zone. Unfortunately, being in this zone is no guarantee of a planetary paradise.

A planet that sits comfortably in the habitable zone is ultimately still at the mercy of its star. Stars emit more than just the light we see. Ultraviolet radiation and X-rays can interact with planetary atmospheres, altering chemistry and even contributing to the loss of the atmosphere.

Violent stellar flares can produce bursts of high-energy radiation. A planet could offer a wonderfully balmy temperature, only to be bombarded with radiation from the host star.

The age of a star matters too. Young stars tend to be more active, with radiation that could erode a planet’s atmosphere over time. Meanwhile, as stars age and evolve, their changing luminosities can drastically alter the conditions a planet experiences.

For the stranded crew in “Exoplanet”, understanding the star overhead may therefore be as important as knowing the landscape beneath their feet.

Even if a planet has survived all the complications we’ve just thrown at it, there’s still a rather important question: could we actually breathe the air?

In "Exoplanet", one crew member discovers that the planet’s atmosphere contains enough oxygen for him to breathe without his spacesuit. On Earth, oxygen makes up around 21% of our atmosphere. However, finding a similar percentage on another planet wouldn’t automatically mean the air is safe to breathe.

Atmospheric pressure matters just as much. At high altitudes on Earth, the oxygen percentage remains the same, but the lower pressure means less oxygen is available to our lungs.

Oxygen isn’t the only concern, either. The fictional planet’s volcanic landscape releases gases such as sulfur dioxide and hydrogen sulfide, both of which are hazardous at high concentrations. Even an oxygen-rich atmosphere could still be lethal.

But what if you don't just need to know if a planet is habitable? What if you need to know if something already lives there?

Before the Wild Blue Yonder arrived at the planet, probes detected dimethyl sulfide, a molecule we associate with biological activity on Earth. This is a beautifully timely detail, as this very molecule has been at the heart of a scientific debate about one of the most intriguing exoplanets discovered so far.

Astronomers have investigated possible signs of dimethyl sulfide in the atmosphere of K2-18 b, an exoplanet orbiting a red dwarf. However, both the detection itself and the biological interpretation remain heavily disputed.

The challenge is that detecting a single molecule isn’t definitive evidence of life. Its presence can be influenced by factors other than biology, like atmospheric chemistry and stellar radiation.

Understanding whether such a molecule could actually indicate life means studying not just the planet’s atmosphere, but also the radiation it receives from its star.

Astronomers have discovered thousands of exoplanets. Understanding whether any could support life is, however, an extraordinary challenge.

The distance between the planet and the star might give us a good clue, but its atmosphere, geology, and stellar environment are all interconnected in determining whether life could survive. For the unfortunate crew of the Wild Blue Yonder, those distinctions could mean the difference between survival and utter disaster.

We might one day identify a planet with Earth-like temperatures, liquid water, and abundant oxygen. But even then, there’s still a huge gap dividing a potentially habitable planet from one where we could safely remove our helmets.

"Exoplanet" is available now exclusively from Audible. It is free for members of its subscription tiers or available to purchase separately.

One crew. One planet. Three timelines. No one survives alone. A sci-fi thriller executive produced by Andy Weir (#1 bestselling author of Project Hail Mary, The Martian), with an all-star cast led by Anthony Mackie, Ariana DeBose and John Leguizamo.

Exoplanet is free for Audible members, but also available for purchase separately.

Alix is an astrophysicist at the University of Birmingham specialising in the characterisation of stars and exoplanets. She is passionate about science communication, and how astrophysics is portrayed in movies, TV, and pop culture at large.
