Analysis: Is Nvidia too big to fail? Its AI financing web raises the stakes

📅 2026-11-11  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The swirl of sex-and-spying allegations around Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang is raising a worrying question: Could trouble at the chipmaker spread through the tightly connected financial network Nvidia has built to support the artificial intelligence boom?

Serious problems with Nvidia could do far-reaching economic and financial damage. The company has made hundreds of billions of dollars in financial commitments to other firms to help the industry grow, earning it the nickname the "central bank of AI." The Federal Reserve and other financial institutions are closely watching the AI buildout to monitor for the possibility that an unexpected event could destabilize the global financial system.

Even back in July Fed officials were discussing the possibility of of a "broad-based repricing of assets" in the event of problems in the AI sector.

A rerun of the 2007-2008 financial crisis isn't likely, according to analysts and current and former financial officials. Nvidia isn't "too big to fail in a bank sense at all," said Sheila Bair, a former government regulator who ran the Federal Deposit Insurance Corp. Unlike a troubled bank, Nvidia isn't vulnerable to a sudden loss of confidence and would still produce a product customers want to buy even if something went wrong. And its massive equity gives it a cushion.

But regulators need to watch carefully for exposure by the banks and the wider financial system to Nvidia and other firms like it, Bair said.

"Nvidia going down would be a pretty seismic event," she said.

Investors still have high hopes for Nvidia, which reports earnings on Tuesday. Analysts expect its revenue to more than double in the current fiscal year, and then to roughly double again over the following two years, according to LSEG data. But with Huang under scrutiny by the FBI, Congress, and Nvidia's board over his relationship with a woman who The New York Times reported was suspected of spying for China, analysts see a wide range of outcomes as suddenly possible, including the potential removal of Huang, who co-founded the company in 1993.

The company didn't immediately respond to a request for comment. Nvidia's board has expressed confidence in its CEO.

Nvidia's stock fell 30% from its Oct. 6 peak through Tuesday's close, amid a broader reassessment of the AI trade prompted by Democrats' surprising victory in the Nov. 3 midterms. Its stock recovered Wednesday, rising more than 4% as of midday. Still, people who closely track the financial flows underlying AI say that even sharper losses by Nvidia likely wouldn't trigger the kind of systemic problems that regulators guard against.

And yet the risks flowing from AI are keeping some finance officials up at night. They are watching for the possibility that many of the factors that have helped the AI boom could reverse simultaneously. If the value of Nvidia's GPU chips fell, for instance, it could prompt investors to ask whether companies that borrowed money to buy them would be able to pay off their debts.

In that case, the financial assistance big tech firms have extended to others in the industry could suddenly become drags on their growth. Tech companies would have credit problems at the same time that their equity is falling. Problems among giant tech firms wouldn't stay contained to tech investors' portfolios, but could cause problems across the economy.

"I think that's a real concern because both the economy and financial markets are standing on the same leg, which means a drawdown could have reinforcing effects between the two," said Jason Wu, an assistant director at the International Monetary Fund who oversees the Global Markets Analysis unit.

Wu was the lead author of a chapter of the IMF's Global Financial Stability report published in October that warned about the potential for growing leverage among AI firms and investors to trigger broader problems. The so-called hyperscalers — the biggest tech firms — have grown from just 1% of bond issuance in the $15 trillion investment-grade debt market to 10% in just two years. Hedge funds and retail investors are making risky bets even riskier by taking on debt to buy stocks or investing in funds that have embedded leverage.

Nvidia is at the center of all of that. The company has repeatedly stepped in to help backstop smaller AI companies known as neoclouds, which build data centers using Nvidia chips and lease capacity to other firms. Among other obligations, Nvidia disclosed in July that it has committed to buy up to $36 billion in cloud services from those companies. That helps Nvidia ensure that there will be more customers for its AI chips, and it helps those companies find easier financing.

Wu and others refer to these kinds of arrangements as circular financing. When business is growing, it can be productive for bigger firms to help their customers buy their products. But if Nvidia couldn't honor its obligations, it would ripple through the firms that borrowed the money and the banks that lent to it. Relatively opaque private credit funds have also lent heavily into the AI boom, and how they would fare is a subject of debate among regulators.

Nvidia didn't reveal its specific partners, but some neoclouds such as CoreWeave and IREN have separately disclosed their relationships with the bigger firm.

Many neoclouds have been under stress in recent weeks. The cost of insuring CoreWeave debt, as measured by credit-default spreads, rose by 94% — nearly doubling — from Oct. 1 to Nov. 5, before settling, according to LSEG data. The price of an IREN convertible bond maturing in 2029 fell 23% from Nov. 3 to Nov. 5, amid a 26% decline in the company's share price.

Nvidia's other obligations include a $105 billion phased guarantee to a major data center project in Ohio planned by OpenAI and SB Energy, subject to certain conditions. Nvidia promised to backstop up to $125 billion of a $500 billion credit program by Wall Street financial firms.

These obligations come in different forms and are unlikely to come due all at once, if ever. Stijn Van Nieuwerburgh, a Columbia Business School finance professor, estimates them at $400 billion overall.

That is a huge sum, but still a small fraction of the $4 trillion market capitalization Nvidia has even after its stock decline. "The company would have to get to the brink of extinction before all these guarantees come into jeopardy."

"I don't want to say it's a safe system, but I do want to say there's a lot of equity in the system right now," Van Nieuwerburgh said.

But in the past few days, Nvidia's troubles have separated it from the pack. Most neoclouds' equity prices have recovered this week as Nvidia's share price has fallen. That suggests investors see Nvidia's problems as specific to the company, for now.

Meanwhile the basic laws of economics are driving other firms to try to move into Nvidia's lucrative chips business. AMD, Broadcom, Intel, Google, Microsoft, and OpenAI all have efforts underway to build their own AI chips, Van Nieuwerburgh said.

Over time, they could replace Nvidia's products. That could cap the economic fallout of any potential troubles at the company.

And yet it's impossible to entirely write off the financial risks of the AI trade suddenly hitting a wall, added Van Nieuwerburgh, who spoke to me over Zoom from the sidelines of a bimonthly meeting of finance officials in Basel, Switzerland.

Central bankers "are clearly very, very, very anxious about this and thinking hard about these questions," he said.

Just like the broader crypto market, memecoins have struggled since the October 2025 crash.

As a whole, the memecoin market has lost roughly $47 billion (65% of the market’s value) since the crash. PEPE and SHIB have also been hit by the bear market.

Using September 30, 2025, as the baseline, PEPE has lost roughly $1.58 billion in market cap, dropping from $3.84 billion to $2.26 billion. Meanwhile, SHIB has lost roughly $4.45 billion as it fell from $7.05 billion to $2.60 billion.

To put this into perspective, PEPE’s market cap has fallen 40%, while SHIB’s has fallen 60%. With this increasing performance gap, many are speculating that PEPE will overtake SHIB soon. However, the key question is whether PEPE is showing stronger momentum or simply benefiting from SHIB’s weakness.

According to AMBCrypto, the answer to this question could determine PEPE’s chances of flipping SHIB.

The logic is simple: The above data highlights a clear performance gap between the two memecoins. SHIB has clearly taken more bearish pressure, closing October more than 60% down from its then peak of $0.000012.

PEPE, on the other hand, has only fallen 44% from $0.000009, while recovering more than 60% from its early Q3 2026 bottom of $0.000003.

This suggests Pepe [PEPE] is showing stronger relative strength, while SHIB continues to face deeper downside pressure. However, the real question will be if the memecoin can continue its momentum and generate enough buying pressure to help it close its market cap deficit.

PEPE has had a busy few weeks since mid-October.

On the 18th, the memecoin rose 4.7%, as a wallet linked with Tron founder Justin Sun received 7.2 trillion tokens. Arkham later found that nearly 3% of the total supply was held in Sun-linked wallets, making him the largest individual holder. On the 22nd of October, PEPE rose by 6% as futures volume neared $900 million.

However, the momentum was also tested by heavy selling. On the 30th of October, the attacker who hacked Binance sold off 7.77 trillion PEPE worth around $31.7 million. Despite the large sale, PEPE’s price only briefly dropped before buyers snapped up the token. More recently, on the 9th of November, surged over 4% in just one hour, adding to signs of renewed trading activity.

The key takeaway? This move came just as the market faced more than $1 billion in liquidations.

In essence, PEPE’s strength is not limited to its performance against SHIB. Instead, it is gaining ground against the broader market. As the chart above shows, the PEPE/BTC ratio has managed to break through a key level of resistance despite the ongoing market FUD, reinforcing PEPE’s relative strength against Bitcoin.

According to AMBCrypto, this indicates a bullish divergence for the memecoin. Increased trading volume, combined with improving technical strength adds further weight to this trend. If PEPE can maintain its upwards trajectory and continue to outperform Shiba Inu [SHIB], it could eventually overtake it to become the second-largest memecoin by market cap before Q4 ends.

The third largest memecoin has held strong despite heavy selling and a wider crypto market crash.

<a href="https://www.fiercehealthcare.com/payers/caresource-launches-1m-grant-program-support-ai-powered-solutions" hreflang="en">CareSource launches $1M grant program to support AI-powered solutions</a>

📅 2026-11-11  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →
CareSource launches $1M grant program to support AI-powered solutions" style="max-width:100%; height:auto; border-radius:8px;" loading="lazy">

Nonprofit insurer CareSource is launching a $1 million program that aims to back responsible AI solutions in healthcare.

The initiative, launched through the CareSource Foundation, is seeking proposals for ways to advance and identify deployments for AI that can drive better outcomes and address barriers for individuals enrolled in Medicaid or who otherwise struggle to access care.

The program will provide up to $1 million over the course of two years to ensure the tool can deliver on meaningful and scalable benefits for the communities it serves.

Erhardt Preitauer, CEO of CareSource, said in an email to Fierce Healthcare that AI offers the potential to improve not just patient care but the experience for providers too as they navigate complex conditions.

"I'm a big believer that when technology, healthcare and the people in our communities rally around a shared purpose, magic can happen," said Preitauer. "Leaving no stone unturned is the only way we can make healthcare work better for people, and AI gives us the power to explore every possibility."

The program will include two phases. First, interested applicants can submit shorter proposals, with the goal of generating interest. Applications that are selected to move on will be tasked with submitting a more fleshed out proposal, including details on implementation, AI governance and sustainability.

CareSource is aiming to identify solutions that ready for implementation and has the potential for scale. Potential focus areas include care coordination, member engagement, behavioral health and rural healthcare. Solutions should use AI to support care delivery but it should not replace human judgement or oversight, the insurer said.

To be eligible, a qualified nonprofit organization must be leading the effort, according to the announcement. Technology companies, academic researchers, healthcare organizations and community-based groups can also play a role as partners.

Applicants moving to the second phase of the program will also be required to showcase data privacy and security, solutions to address and prevent bias and ongoing performance tracking.

<a href="https://www.fiercehealthcare.com/digital-health/maven-clinic-takes-new-dtc-vertical-network-475-commercial-plans" hreflang="en">Maven Clinic takes new DTC vertical in-network with 475+ commercial plans</a>

📅 2026-11-11  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →
Maven Clinic takes new DTC vertical in-network with 475+ commercial plans" style="max-width:100%; height:auto; border-radius:8px;" loading="lazy">

Maven Clinic, a virtual provider for women’s and family health, is making its new DTC vertical in-network with over 475 commercial health plans.

The first program to be in-network, starting now, is hormone care. Starting on Jan. 1, 2027, its GLP-1 program will also be covered. The fee-for-service contracts represent about 80% of commercially insured lives, according to the company. It is able to tap into the vast network through direct contracts and a third-party partner that it can’t name on the record. Coverage and applicable copays will vary by plan.

“We’re really doing this because of access [gaps] … the in-network coverage removes that barrier,” Alex Kubal, general manager of Maven's Consumer Division, told Fierce Healthcare.

Also in 2027, Maven plans to roll out in its DTC vertical, several additional specialties, including OBGYN care, maternal-fetal medicine, nurse practitioners for general women’s health and nutrition. Employees can use their health plan to get care for PMOS, endometriosis, hormonal, sexual or metabolic health, regardless of whether or not their employer sponsors a Maven program. Cash pay will also remain available.

“We need to be able to maintain pathways for both,” Kubal said. “We want people to get access to high-quality clinical care, which we see as a gap in the market.”

For example, treating perimenopause symptoms remains a challenge due to a lack of standardized diagnostics. “What we’re trying to do is really educate women and make it a safe place for women to come and talk about what they’re experiencing, and get high quality clinical care, so they don’t have to suffer through it,” Kubal said.

The DTC business, which launched six months ago, “is seeing really great traction from consumers,” per Kubal. It has grown by 55% a month, on average, in terms of subscriptions. In October, its GLP-1 program grew 80% from the prior month. While government payers are part of the discussion, going in-network with them is not on Maven's immediate roadmap, Kubal said.

<a href="https://www.fiercehealthcare.com/ai-and-machine-learning/amazon-web-services-commits-100m-ai-cloud-tech-initiatives-over-next-5" hreflang="en">Amazon Web Services commits $100M to AI, cloud tech health initiatives over next 5 years</a>

📅 2026-11-11  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →
Amazon Web Services commits $100M to AI, cloud tech health initiatives over next 5 years" style="max-width:100%; height:auto; border-radius:8px;" loading="lazy">

Amazon Web Services (AWS) is committing $100 million towards artificial intelligence, agentic services and cloud infrastructures for organizations to close healthcare gaps across the globe.

The funds are part of the AWS Health Impact Initiative (HII), a five-year program, announced Monday.

While “agents and AI are revolutionizing” healthcare services, communities that need them often miss out if organizations don’t know how to access them, AWS Chief Medical Officer and Director of Healthcare and Life Sciences Rowland Illing, M.D., said in a statement.

“The AWS Health Impact Initiative helps organizations that support those communities to build with AI and extends our commitment to democratize AI and cloud-powered health innovations that benefit everyone, everywhere,” Illing said.

HII funds projects based on three main domains: expanding access to healthcare services, improving access to healthy food and advancing women’s and children’s health research and innovation.

AWS says the domains were informed by customer feedback, peer-reviewed research and AWS’ social impact investments. The company highlighted the AWS Health Equity Initiative (HEI), which was launched in 2021 and provided $60 million towards AI and cloud technology to more than 400 awardees.

Since 2021, AWS has supported over 600 health organizations in more than 80 countries, executives say.

Organizations, ranging from healthcare nonprofits to government agencies to research institutions, can apply for financial support on the AWS website.

Applications will be reviewed weekly, the company says.

On-Device Speech Recognition and Text-to-Speech in Google AI Edge Gallery

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Today, we are introducing on-device Automatic Speech Recognition (ASR) and Text-to-Speech (TTS) capabilities in Google AI Edge Gallery. Voice is often the most natural way to interact with AI on phones and wearables. Recent advances in compact speech architectures now make it possible to run accurate, multilingual ASR and expressive TTS models entirely on device, all within a small memory footprint, with low latency, and completely offline. By pairing these lightweight speech models with on-device small language models (SLMs) like Gemini Nano and Gemma 4, developers can build apps that stream live transcriptions as the user speaks and synthesize natural spoken replies locally.

This release brings together major advancements across both Google’s system speech models and the open-weight ecosystem:

Below, we’ll explore how this local voice pipeline powers the new XR (Extended Reality) Language Teacher showcase, upgrades AI Chat in Google AI Edge Gallery, and how you can build with these APIs and models today.

Learning a language while traveling happens in the real world, often in underground stations, busy markets, or abroad where Wi-Fi and roaming data may not be available. To show how vision, language, and speech models can work together 100% locally on your phone with no network connection needed, we built the XR Language Teacher showcase in Google AI Edge Gallery. Simply point your camera at an unfamiliar object or menu, ask "What is that, and how do I ask for one?", and immediately hear an explanation in your language alongside authentic pronunciation in the local language.

Link to Youtube Video (visible only when JS is disabled)

With inference running locally on a paired phone, the same experience works standalone on a phone and extends naturally to hands-free smart glasses. To see how we adapted Language Teacher for intelligent glasses check out our recent talk at Android Dev Summit: Building Audio-First Experiences for Intelligent Eyewear with Gemini Nano.

Beyond the XR Language Teacher showcase, we also built ASR and TTS directly into Google AI Edge Gallery’s main AI Chat feature, giving on-device SLMs like Gemini Nano and Gemma 4 a natural, offline voice interface while on the go.

Link to Youtube Video (visible only when JS is disabled)

While multimodal models like Gemma 4 can process audio inputs directly, we provide a modular solution for cascading language models with separate, lightweight voice models for latency optimization and flexibility. Depending on your target devices and model requirements, our open-source code on GitHub shows you the optimized and recommended solution for Android using ML Kit, as well as MediaPipe Tasks and LiteRT for cross-platform coverage and open model support.

For developers building for Android devices, ML Kit’s GenAI Speech Recognition and Text-to-Speech APIs provide a standardized, platform-integrated path out of the box. With the underlying speech models managed through AICore and Google’s on-device speech services, developers get production-grade quality without having to manage model downloads or worry about APK size bloat. Your app also benefits automatically from hardware acceleration across NPU and TPU backends (when available) and seamless background model updates.

Integrating ASR and TTS into cross-platform applications usually requires handling low-level audio framing, Voice Activity Detection (VAD), and complex decoding pipelines. MediaPipe Task simplifies this by providing unified, turnkey ASR and TTS APIs across iOS, macOS, Windows, Linux, and Web that abstract away manual pre-processing, audio stream routing, and platform-specific audio management into simple, high-level interfaces:

Under the hood, ML Kit and MediaPipe Tasks are powered by LiteRT, Google’s high-performance on-device AI inference infrastructure. Cross-platform applications requiring even more fine-grained control over speech processing or custom model architectures can also build on it directly.

Speech models often rely on multiple subgraphs and auxiliary assets, such as encoder/decoder subgraphs and tokenizers for ASR, or phonemizer dictionaries, voice embeddings, and vocoders for TTS. All required components for each model are packaged into a single .litertlm file for easy deployment. The app ships with ready-to-use .litertlm models, including Parakeet-TDT-0.6B-v3 (multilingual ASR), PocketTTS 100M (multilingual TTS), Phonon 31M (ultra-lightweight English TTS), and Kokoro-82M (multilingual TTS). Beyond these, the vibrant Hugging Face LiteRT Community with over 10K+ followers offers a growing catalog of open-weight models, like Whisper, Qwen3-ASR and Moonshine for ASR, or Qwen3-TTS and Matcha for TTS. For developers seeking custom implementations or support for additional open-weight voice models, LiteRT provides a rich set of multimodal model examples to help you get started, with more on the way.

Adding a local voice pipeline takes on-device SLMs beyond typing-based chat interfaces, enabling natural, voice-first interactions that work anywhere, completely offline. All the building blocks to try these experiences or integrate them into your own apps are available today:

We'd like to extend a special thanks to our significant contributors for their work on this project:

Adobe Connect APSB26-191: Six CVSS 10.0s, Five Branches, One Suffix That Breaks Your Scanner

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Posted on Nov 11 • Originally published at threataft.com

Adobe dropped 24 CVEs in Adobe Connect on November 10. Eighteen are Critical. Six are CVSS 10.0.

Every one of the six 10.0s shares the same vector: AV:N/AC:L/PR:N/UI:N/S:C/C:H/I:H/A:N. Network-reachable, no authentication, no user interaction, scope changed. No precondition an attacker has to work around.

Two are confirmed by their own CVE records: CVE-2026-121356 and CVE-2026-121889, both SSRF.

The other four sit across path traversal, XXE, code injection, and SQL injection — six different flaw classes landing on the same score. That's not a cluster. That's a codebase where input validation was inconsistent, and a researcher (edra, credited for 15 of the 24) found every place it wasn't enforced.

Adobe provides five fixed builds, one per branch:

A server on 12.12 with no suffix is still vulnerable. A patch scanner that compares integers (>= 12.12) will show green. The suffix is the version.

This matters if you're writing detection logic, compliance checks, or SIEM rules against installed software versions. String comparison on the full version — including the alphabetic suffix — is the only correct check.

None of these is an exotic class. All of them are in the OWASP Top 10. The finding is that a mature, shipping product hadn't applied consistent defenses across its own surface.

Adobe states it's not aware of exploits in the wild, and nothing is in CISA KEV as of November 11. That's accurate as of today.

A 10.0 SSRF with no auth requirement is a short research project to weaponize. Seven SQL injections on an internet-facing meeting server are automatable with off-the-shelf tooling. "Not aware of exploitation" is a timestamp.

Priority order, hunt queries, and mitigation checklist: 👉 https://threataft.com/articles/adobe-connect-apsb26-191-mass-disclosure-24-cves

The Agentic Shift: How Autonomous AI Agents Are Transforming Human Resources

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The Agentic Shift: How Autonomous AI Agents Are Transforming Human Resources

Human Resources is experiencing a profound architectural shift. While the first wave of enterprise artificial intelligence focused on generative text and passive chatbots—answering standard FAQ questions or drafting job descriptions—the modern workplace is embracing agentic AI. Unlike traditional automation that follows rigid if-then rules, AI agents possess contextual reasoning, autonomy, and multi-step execution capabilities. In HR, autonomous agents are evolving from administrative assistants into active operational partners that streamline recruitment, personalize employee experiences, and proactively support talent retention.

Recruiting has historically suffered from manual screening bottlenecks and fragmented applicant communication. Agentic workflows fundamentally redesign this funnel:

Dynamic Candidate Sourcing: Instead of waiting for applicants or running static boolean keyword searches, agentic sourcing systems continuously analyze industry repositories, professional networks, and open-source contributions. They evaluate technical competencies, trajectory, and cultural alignment against real-time workforce planning data.

Autonomous Pre-Screening and Scheduling: Sourcing agents interact with candidates conversationally across messaging channels, verifying prerequisite qualifications, handling mutual calendar coordination, and answering candidate questions about compensation benchmarks and team culture 24/7.

Objective Candidate Shortlisting: By anonymizing candidate profiles during early assessment rounds and focusing strictly on verifiable project outcomes and technical assessments, AI agents reduce unconscious bias in initial talent screening.

Traditional onboarding often overwhelms new hires with compliance checklists, disparate portal logins, and static policy documents. An onboarding agent acts as a dedicated 90-day digital concierge:

Workflow Orchestration: The agent coordinates across IT, facilities, and department heads to provision hardware, assign software licenses, and schedule introductory peer check-ins before Day One.

Contextual Knowledge Retrieval: Rather than forcing employees to search hundreds of pages in an intranet wiki, the agent answers nuanced inquiries—such as healthcare tier eligibility, 401(k) vesting timelines, or travel reimbursement policies—tailored specifically to the employee's jurisdiction and job level.

Check-In Cadence: The agent proactively prompts new hires at days 7, 30, and 60 to gauge onboarding satisfaction, identify learning friction, and notify team leads when extra support is needed.

Annual performance reviews are notoriously backward-looking and time-consuming. Agentic HR tools shift organizations toward continuous coaching and internal mobility:

Real-Time Feedback Synthesis: Agents analyze structured feedback loops across project management tools, peer acknowledgments, and goal milestones to provide managers with objective performance summaries ahead of 1-on-1 coaching sessions.

Dynamic Skills Gap Analysis: As market requirements evolve, talent agents cross-reference employee project histories with company strategic priorities, autonomously recommending targeted learning paths and internal gig opportunities.

Proactive Career Pathing: Employees receive personalized recommendations for lateral transfers, certifications, and mentorship pairings that match their long-term professional aspirations.

Employee turnover often catches leadership by surprise. Autonomous agents provide early-warning signals while respecting personal data boundaries:

Anonymized Engagement Pulse: Instead of relying solely on annual engagement surveys, agents analyze aggregated sentiment across anonymized pulse surveys and workplace communications to detect team-level burnout or cultural friction points.

Predictive Flight-Risk Triggers: By correlating macro indicators—such as stagnant tenure in grade, market compensation divergence, and project fatigue—agents alert HR business partners to intervene proactively with tailored retention or role-refresh conversations.

With autonomous agency comes the vital responsibility of ethical stewardship. Organizations deploying HR agents must maintain strict governance guardrails:

Human-in-the-Loop (HITL) for High-Stakes Decisions: AI agents should recommend, summarize, and execute administrative workflows, but all consequential personnel decisions—such as hiring, termination, disciplinary action, and compensation adjustments—must remain strictly in the hands of human leaders.

Algorithmic Transparency & Auditability: Every decision tree, ranking score, and recommendation made by an agent must be auditable, explainable, and compliant with emerging employment regulations (such as NYC Local Law 144 and EU AI Act employment standards).

Data Privacy and Segregation: Employee personal data, medical leaves, and performance notes must be strictly partitioned with enterprise-grade encryption and role-based access control, ensuring external language models are never trained on proprietary organizational data.

The emergence of agentic AI does not eliminate the human element in Human Resources; rather, it liberates HR professionals from routine operational overhead. By deploying autonomous agents to handle repetitive coordination, continuous data aggregation, and real-time support, HR leaders can reclaim their essential role as strategic advisors, culture builders, and empathetic mentors. Organizations that balance agentic efficiency with rigorous ethical oversight will build the most agile, resilient, and human-centric workforces of the future.

How small shops in Sri Lanka can go digital on a small budget: POS, website, WhatsApp orders

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Most small shops I talk to in Sri Lanka don't need an "app". They need three boring things to work: customers can find them, customers can order without a phone call that gets missed, and the owner knows what sold and what's left at the end of the day.

You can get all three without a big upfront bill. Here's the order I'd do it in, with free options first.

Disclosure: I build two of the tools mentioned below (goko.lk and webdev.lk) with my team at KDJ Lanka. I've listed other options next to them, and every step works without my tools.

Before a website, before anything, claim your Google Business Profile. Google says it plainly: "creating a Business Profile and listing your business on Google is free."

Then ask your regular customers for a review. Don't buy reviews and don't write fake ones. A few honest ones are worth more.

Your customers are already on WhatsApp. The mistake is running a business from a personal account where orders get buried between family chats.

The WhatsApp Business app is built for this. From Meta's own product page, it gives you:

If a customer pays by bank transfer, check the money in your bank app before you hand over goods. Never trust a screenshot alone. Payment fraud in Sri Lanka is growing, and fake slips are the easy version.

This is where most shops lose money quietly: an item runs out and nobody notices, or credit customers ("naya") pile up in a book that only one person can read.

The cloud option I build is goko.lk. Its free plan covers real billing, stock and customer records at Rs. 0, with no credit card and no trial countdown. The Pro plan adds things like a custom domain and 24/7 email support. Whatever you pick, look for these four things:

Start by entering only your fast-moving items. You can add the rest over a month.

You don't need a website on day one. Google Business Profile plus WhatsApp covers most of what a small shop needs. A website starts to make sense when you want:

Your options range from free builders where you drag and drop sections yourself, to a freelancer or agency, to AI builders.

Full disclosure again: my team built WebDev LK for this. You describe your business out loud in Sinhala, Tamil or English, and it builds and publishes a site to a free yourname.webdev.lk address with SSL. The free plan allows 5 AI changes a day, and Pro is LKR 1,000/month or LKR 5,000/year if you want a custom domain. Whatever tool you use, keep it simple: one page with what you sell, where you are, your hours, and a WhatsApp button.

Going digital isn't about buying the most features. It's about not losing customers you already have: the ones who couldn't find your hours, whose WhatsApp order got buried, or who left because the item they wanted had quietly run out.

Do steps 1 and 2 this week. They cost nothing. Add a POS when counting stock starts eating your evenings.

If you run a small shop in Sri Lanka, what's the one thing that wastes the most of your time right now? Tell me in the comments.

Building a Smart Supply Chain Intelligence Platform on AWS

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

What if your business could identify inventory shortages before they happen, understand supplier performance, and track procurement spending, all from one intelligent platform?

Imagine managing a company that operates across multiple warehouses.

Your procurement team is placing purchase orders. Warehouse teams are receiving and dispatching products. Sales teams are tracking customer demand. Suppliers are providing delivery updates and pricing information.

Every department generates valuable data, but that information often lives in separate systems.

The procurement manager has one report. The warehouse manager maintains another spreadsheet. Finance uses a separate application, while sales data is stored somewhere else.

When management asks a simple question—How much inventory do we actually have, which suppliers are delivering late, and where are we spending the most money?—getting a reliable answer becomes difficult.

This is where a Smart Supply Chain Intelligence Platform on AWS can help.

Instead of treating procurement, inventory, suppliers, sales, and warehouses as separate data silos, we can bring them together into a centralized data platform and turn disconnected business information into actionable intelligence.

Let's explore how to design this solution using AWS services.

A modern supply chain depends on multiple business systems.

An ERP system may contain purchase orders and financial transactions. A warehouse management system tracks stock movements. A procurement application manages suppliers and purchasing activities. A sales platform records customer orders and product demand.

Each system provides only part of the picture.

A company sells electronic products through several warehouses.

Without connected data, the company might discover the shortage only when customers start experiencing delays.

A centralized intelligence platform can combine inventory, sales, purchase orders, and supplier performance to give decision-makers a clearer picture.

The goal is not simply to build another dashboard. It is to create a trusted data foundation for better operational decisions.

The platform will centralize five important business areas:

All these areas will use standardized datasets stored in a centralized AWS data lake.

The architecture follows a layered data lake approach.

Business data arrives from different systems, is stored in Amazon S3, transformed using AWS Glue, governed through AWS Lake Formation, queried with Amazon Athena, and presented through Amazon QuickSight.

AWS Step Functions coordinates processing workflows, while AWS Lambda supports lightweight event-driven operations.

This is a reference architecture. The actual ingestion method and processing frequency depend on source systems and business requirements.

Amazon S3 provides centralized storage for the platform.

We can organize the data into three logical layers:

This follows the medallion architecture concept.

Stores original source data with minimal modification.

This provides traceability and allows data to be reprocessed when transformation logic changes.

Contains standardized and validated datasets.

Contains curated datasets designed for reporting and business analysis.

For analytical workloads, Apache Parquet is a useful storage format because it is columnar and works well with Amazon Athena.

We can also partition datasets based on common query patterns:

Good partitioning can reduce unnecessary data scanning. However, too many small partitions or files can hurt performance.

Data from different business systems rarely follows the same structure.

One system might identify a product as PROD-1001, while another uses 1001.

Supplier names, dates, currencies, column names, and data types may also differ.

AWS Glue helps process these datasets and transform them into consistent structures.

This is an illustrative transformation, not a complete production Glue job. A production implementation also needs schema validation, invalid-record handling, deduplication, partition management, and a suitable write strategy.

Different supply chain datasets may arrive at different frequencies.

AWS Step Functions can coordinate multi-stage processing workflows.

AWS Lambda can handle lightweight tasks such as validating incoming file metadata or starting a workflow.

AWS Glue performs the larger data transformations, while Step Functions coordinates the stages and their error-handling paths.

Amazon CloudWatch can monitor workflow executions, Glue job failures, and processing duration.

A data lake is useful only when users can discover datasets and access them securely.

AWS Glue Data Catalog maintains metadata such as:

Glue Crawlers can help discover schemas, although production environments should use controlled schema-management practices.

AWS Lake Formation provides centralized data lake governance.

Lake Formation supports fine-grained permissions for supported integrations, helping organizations manage access at database, table, column, row, and cell levels.

Centralized data should not mean unrestricted data access.

Once datasets are cleansed, cataloged, and available in S3, Amazon Athena allows analysts to query them using SQL.

This calculates inventory value by warehouse, assuming the dataset contains compatible quantity and unit-cost values.

This helps identify products that may require replenishment.

This calculates on-time delivery among records with an actual delivery date.

A production supplier scorecard should also define how partial deliveries, cancelled orders, revised delivery dates, and incomplete records are handled.

These queries are starting points. Actual table and column names must match the curated datasets in the Glue Data Catalog.

SQL queries are useful for analysts, but business managers often need visual dashboards.

Amazon QuickSight can turn curated analytical datasets into interactive reports.

The platform could provide several dashboards.

Sales teams can explore revenue, product demand, and order fulfillment. Warehouse managers can investigate inventory distribution, receiving and dispatch volumes, stock transfers, and warehouse-level shortages.

The key is to define business metrics consistently so that different dashboards do not report conflicting results.

A procurement team should not evaluate suppliers only by purchase price.

A supplier offering a lower price may also have longer delivery times or higher product rejection rates.

For example, an illustrative scorecard might show:

These are sample figures, not real supplier performance results.

A supplier's overall performance should not be reduced to one number without considering order size, product category, delivery location, and contract terms.

Too little inventory can lead to lost sales and delayed orders.

Too much inventory can increase storage costs and tie up working capital.

A centralized data platform can help answer important questions:

When available inventory approaches this level, the platform can flag the product for replenishment review.

More advanced inventory policies may also consider demand variability, seasonal patterns, service-level targets, minimum order quantities, and supplier constraints.

The core architecture is a centralized data lake and analytics platform. It is suitable for historical reporting, supplier scorecards, and many procurement and inventory analyses.

Some businesses, however, need more frequent inventory updates.

Imagine a warehouse employee scanning a barcode or RFID tag whenever a product is received, moved, or dispatched.

A possible real-time extension could look like this:

AWS IoT Core can support connected-device ingestion, while Amazon Kinesis Data Streams can handle event streams.

This extension complements the data lake rather than replacing it. Historical and curated datasets can still be stored in S3 and analyzed through Athena.

A production supply chain platform needs more than dashboards and SQL queries.

Validate incoming datasets before publishing them to the analytics layer.

Check for missing fields, invalid identifiers, duplicate records, unexpected schema changes, and reconciliation differences.

Keep rejected records in a controlled quarantine area for investigation.

Use Step Functions to coordinate workflows and define retries, timeouts, and failure-handling paths.

Make ingestion idempotent so repeated files or API responses do not inflate business metrics.

Use CloudWatch to monitor Glue job failures, workflow executions, processing duration, data freshness, and operational errors.

Dashboard users should be able to see when the underlying data was last refreshed.

The goal is to build a reliable platform without introducing unnecessary operational complexity.

You don't need to build everything at once.

A phased approach makes the project easier to develop and expand.

Phase 6: Add orchestration and monitoring

Starting with batch analytics provides a manageable foundation. Real-time ingestion and predictive capabilities can be added when business requirements justify them.

Supply chain problems are often data problems.

A business may have inventory information, supplier records, purchase orders, sales history, and warehouse activity. But if these datasets remain disconnected, it becomes difficult to understand what is really happening across the operation.

A Smart Supply Chain Intelligence Platform brings these sources together and creates a shared foundation for operational analytics.

Amazon S3 provides centralized storage. AWS Glue processes and transforms datasets. Glue Data Catalog maintains metadata. Lake Formation helps govern access. Amazon Athena provides SQL analytics, while Amazon QuickSight turns results into interactive dashboards.

The next step can be even more powerful: connecting real-time inventory events, forecasting demand, and identifying supply chain risks earlier.

The future of supply chain management is not just moving products from one place to another. It is using connected data to make smarter decisions at every stage.

With AWS, organizations can start building that intelligence one data pipeline at a time.

Rocket Lab: 3 Key Takeaways for Long-Term Investors

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Electron's 96% mission success rate across nearly 100 flights gives customers a track record to lean on before committing to Neutron's premium pricing.

On its third-quarter 2026 earnings call, held on Nov. 9, 2026, Rocket Lab (RKLB +6.54%) reported quarterly revenue of $268 million, representing 73% year-over-year growth. Global launch supply remains constrained, so spacecraft builders with proven flight hardware can command higher prices. Scaling flight cadence and software capabilities expands the company's addressable market. Three key takeaways from management stand out for long-term investors.

Global launch availability remains tight, so rocket providers can raise launch fees rather than offer early customer discounts. For Neutron, its new medium-lift launch vehicle designed to carry 13 tonnes, customer demand pushed base contract prices higher as early flight slots filled up into the next decade.

Execution consistency matters for satellite operators who can't afford payload delays. Rocket Lab manufactures the Electron, its small orbital launch vehicle, at a rapid pace while maintaining a 96% mission success rate across nearly 100 flights, helping it retain commercial and defense customers.

Expanding beyond launch into constellation management provides predictable recurring revenue. Rocket Lab completed a $2.4 billion at-the-market equity offering program, in which it sells shares in the open market over time.

Launch capacity constraints and satellite constellation build-outs continue to surge, driving factory floors to full operational capacity. In its Space Systems segment, which designs and builds satellites and components, Rocket Lab maintains an operational footprint spanning solar cells, flight software, and satellite subassemblies. Vertically integrated manufacturing shields the company from broader supply chain bottlenecks. Meanwhile, growing backlog, which represents signed customer contracts awaiting delivery, provides long-term revenue visibility while management focuses on launch cadence execution and cost discipline.

Building reusable rocket infrastructure requires substantial early capital investments, but established operational momentum positions the company to capture expanding market share across commercial and national security missions.

Factorial Energy (FAC) Q3 2026 Earnings Call Transcript

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Early aerospace deliveries arrived, but a $1.8 million inventory write-down hit gross results.

Need a quote from a Motley Fool analyst? Email [email protected]

Factorial Energy Inc. (FAC -4.70%) transitioned to a commercial-stage company in the third quarter of 2026, recording its first revenue from solid-state battery cell deliveries to the aerospace market. Management reported reaching an energy density of 450 watt-hours per kilogram for its aerospace cells, meeting its year-end performance target ahead of schedule while achieving a pilot line yield of 85%. The company is executing a fabless business model, forming strategic partnerships with manufacturers like SK On to leverage existing global production capacity for the automotive sector. Factorial revised its full-year spending and capital expenditure guidance downward, citing cost reclassifications and disciplined operational management as it prepares for meaningful revenue growth in 2027.

In part due to a 20% reduction in battery weight before any engineering optimization. The lightest drone group up to 20 pounds at a Game of Drones, passing 'bands' at Edwards Air Force Base in California, with 521 minutes in the air covering roughly 300 miles.

In August, our solid state and lithium metal cells powered the winning aircraft in a DARPA LIFT challenge which carried a payload of nearly 4x its own weight. For drone makers, battery performance is 1 of the fastest levers to better mission capability. These results give customers independent, real world proof that our solid-state delivers as they move from testing towards production. From here, we expect revenue to grow in 3 ways. The first is more customers. We are working to qualify our aerospace products with additional customers starting in October 2027 The department of defense will restrict batteries from named FEOC, or foreign entities of concern.

For manufacturers, qualifying a new cell maker takes months, so drone makers are picking their suppliers now. Our U.S. headquarters and our fabrication in United States and South Korea aligned with that shift. The second is better cells. In a drone, energy density decides how far it flies and how much it carries. Last quarter, we said we expected our dual program sales to move towards approximately 450 watt-hours per kilogram by year- end We have achieved 450 watt-hours per kilogram as of today. About 15% higher energy density than the cells that powered our world record flight this year. We continue to target further improvement next year.

That work builds on the A-samples we delivered to Mercedes Benz, which came in at about 390 watt-hours per kilogram. It shows how much synergy our automotive program brings to our high-power applications. The next job is to carry those gains from development cells into production cells. At consistent quality, because that is what turns the performance lead into orders. Independent testing including nail penetration, crush, and thermal tests has confirmed the safety of the FAST cells we have delivered. Solstice is our all solid state battery platform with no liquid inside. We have built those cells as large as 40-amp-hour, and deliver them to partners for validation. Separate from the drone program.

Targeted, is designed for up to 450 watt-hours per kilogram, and its enhanced thermal stability when compared to conventional lithium ion battery cells, that makes it a fit for robotics, space systems, and other users that need longer cycle life and high thermal stability. The third is automotive. A much bigger market that moves on a longer timeline. It follows the same path as aerospace: partnership, system- level integration, and then a commercial order. Over the years, our work there has grown from 1 vehicle to several, across brands and regions. Mercedes Benz put our results on public roads in 2025. Built them into a Dodge Charger Daytona development vehicle this year.

And right after quarter end, Show the Pujo RCZ version concept car. A solid state battery developed together with Factorio. Our next major milestone in automotive is a production uptake agreement with a mainstream automaker. Building on the supercar order we received in January. An agreement would set us up to ramp towards volume production by the end of the decade. This is how we are different. Partnership system level integration, to commercial order. Each step takes years of effort. Many companies have technologies on paper, in a lab,, but having a claim on a performance is different from having announcements of partnerships with leading customers with substantial investment and support over the years.

Having just partnership is different from having those partners building the vehicles on the ground and in the air. Not only functioning but set the world record. Having those system integration for an innovative product is different from having a commercial order. We are not just living on a promise but the track record. We shared that we will have the first revenue by 2027, and we delivered in Q3 26. Today, we built cells on our pilot line in South Korea, and at our headquarters near Boston. Those lines have shipped thousands of large format cells to automakers for testing, and our lines in Korea can switch between the lithium-metal and silicon versions of FAST.

Next, we are expanding our own lines for FAST by end of 2027 and Solstice by end of 28. The new FAST line is being designed to make commercial cells build to the performance requirements of aerospace and physical AI applications. Then, we plan to scale through partners. We designed FAST so that a line can reach up to 80% of the equipment in professional lab-in-a-line. That lets us grow through our partners' existing factories rather than new ones. It also avoids much of the cost in the environmental impact of our new construction.

In July, SK On, 1 of Korea's leading global battery manufacturers, signed a memorandum of understanding with us to jointly evaluate producing our battery technologies across its global manufacturing network. SKON is our second significant industrialization partner this year, which is major progress towards our fab-less business model. It has more than 200 gigawatt-hours of annual production capacity worldwide, including about 100 gigawatt-hours in the United States. On the supply chain side, in September, we signed a joint development agreement with Mitsui Kinzoku, a leading manufacturer of sulfide solid electrolyte. To help industrialize Solstice. Its solid electrolyte is advancing towards mass production, at a dedicated plant in Saitama, Japan.

This builds on our partnership with Possible Future Anne a leading cathode and anode materials provider in Korea. With Mitsui Kinzoku joining POSCO FUTURE M, we now have partners for all 3 key active materials of Solstice, which position us to move solstice to scale. We are also working with Phil Energy, a leading Korean maker of battery stacking and assembly equipment and 1 of our investors to build out the manufacturing infrastructure for Solstice. Our partnership coalition led by Factorio is designed to match the scale of today's largest cell makers through a fabless model. We believe the market will divide into designers and contract manufacturers.

Our innovation extends beyond materials and technology to engineering, and we bring that expertise to our partners. line to raise yield and quality. Partners can supply us with manufacturing capacity, The know how to make solid state battery work reliably at scale is hard to come by. And that is what we bring: more than 150 patents and patent applications. On cost, our work covers manufacturing, materials, and the supply chain. In manufacturing, yield has a large effect. Because every scrapped cell adds to the cost of the good ones. When we began production of our first technology, yield was low.

Our power line has since demonstrated yield about 85%, Compared with the 70% to 80% that state- of the art lithium-ion battery pilot lines typically target. With everything else held equal, that improvement has cut the cost of every battery cell by more than 80%. Solstice's dry cathode process, also uses less energy and avoids the volatile organic compounds that come with solvent based processing. In materials, our lithium metal cells follow a different cost curve from silicon anode cells. With silicon anodes, more energy density takes more silicon. And the cell gets more expensive. With lithium metal, more energy density comes from a thinner anode, which means less material.

Over time, we believe that can make our lithium-metal cells less expensive than silicon anode cells. And because neither platform needs rare earth metals, we believe our cell cost can approach that of lithium ion and become competitive as volume and our supply chain grow. In the near term, we are focused on holding yield as volumes rise, qualifying our drone products with more customers, and moving Solstice towards industrialization, with POSCO future m. it is taking a call now, Phil Energy, and our manufacturing partners. For now, we see significant opportunities in physical AI, automotive, and energy storage. With that, C. Wei will take you through the numbers and our outlook.

Does that what really matters for the drone industry and for the aerospace? That we are targeting in the near term. A lot of them, they do not really require many cycles. And, I mean, it is not really long cycles that you are looking at for those specific applications. And that is why we have, deemphasized the cycle life requirements and, which is apparent and much smaller than automotive. Environment. But the discharging capabilities or the power capabilities could be 5 to 10x as high as what is required for, the automotive industry. So this is where we have never seen before. But a lithium-metal cell has actually a very unique advantage on that.

Because for lithium ion, especially silicon and graphite lithium ion batteries, you will have a natural compromise between energy and power. Usually, energy cells cannot deliver a high power, and a high power cell will have very limited energy. And when you try to push both, the cost can become a big issue even you are getting remotely close. So that is why we think that for this technology, it will be able to get to the energy density much more quickly because lithium metal is literally the lightest metal on Earth right now. And at the same time, you can push for high power. Without the traditional physical limits. That the lithium ion battery are having.

Sky Harbour (SKYH) Q3 2026 Earnings Call Transcript

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Revenue jumped 45% on new campuses, but EBITDA guidance was lowered.

Need a quote from a Motley Fool analyst? Email [email protected]

Sky Harbour Group Corporation (SKYH -2.75%) reported an expansion of its aviation infrastructure network through new ground leases and construction milestones across high-demand aviation markets. Management indicated that the company is expanding into third-party manufacturing and construction services to maximize the capacity utilization of its internal steel production facility. The company stated that its leasing strategy focuses on tier 1 airports where revenue per square foot potential is highest, while vertical integration of the supply chain serves to mitigate cyclical inflationary pressures. Management reported that the company maintains a fixed-rate debt structure to insulate the business from recent volatility in the interest rate environment.

And hopefully that will come at some point in the next year or so. You definitely see the benefit on the local side. The scarcity dynamic that I just referred to earlier is another part of that. And then lastly, it is just operating leverage, is, you know, again, that OpEx line does not change as your economic occupancy goes up. And, frankly, as your square footage goes up. Tron Inc reports 22% rise in Q3 net income as unrealized staking revenue grows

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

The company reported Q3 2026 net income of $14.9 million, up 22% year-over-year, with a nine-month net income of $39.4 million, up 203%.

TRX Tron (TRX) treasury firm Tron Inc. posted a higher third-quarter net income, as it continued to accumulate TRX tokens and saw rising staking income.

The company booked a net income of $14.9 million in Q3, up 22% year-on-year, according to its latest quarterly report released Tuesday. Its nine-month net income reached $39.4 million, up 203%.

The company's digital asset holdings rose to $257.8 million at the end of September. It increased its TRX holdings to 723 million TRX ($229.3 million) as of Tuesday, according to a post on X.

Notably, its unrealized staking income rose 41% year-over-year to roughly $3.18 million in the third quarter. For the first nine months of the year, its unrealized staking income more than quadrupled to $9.51 million.

"Unlike unrealized gains resulting from changes in digital asset market prices, staking income reflects rewards associated with the company's participation in Tron Network staking activities," the company said.

"Our strategy is expanding beyond holding digital assets to providing additional value-add services for the Tron network. Our election as a TRON Super Representative in October represents an initial step in positioning Tron Inc. for further infrastructure integration within the TRON ecosystem," Tron Inc. CEO Rich Miller said in the report.

Tron Inc. rebranded from SRM Entertainment in July 2025, shifting its focus from toy design and entertainment to crypto treasury management. It brought on Tron founder Justin Sun as an advisor.

TRX, which has a market capitalization of $30.1 billion, traded flat over the past 24 hours at $0.3172, according to The Block's price page. It has declined 5.5% over the last five months but remained up 11.5% year-to-date.

Meanwhile, Nasdaq-listed shares of Tron Inc. closed flat at $1.45 on Tuesday. The stock has risen 17.9% over the past month but remained down 33.5% over the past six months.

‘You’ve Got Mail’ Reboot in the Works at Warner Bros.

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The film from director Jason Orley will be a new take on the original 1998 rom-com starring Tom Hanks and Meg Ryan.

A new take on the beloved rom-com You’ve Got Mail is in the works at Warner Bros., The Hollywood Reporter has learned.

Rather than a remake of the original 1998 film starring Tom Hanks and Meg Ryan, the new film will follow a new story, though plot details are being kept under wraps.

Jason Orley (Close Personal Friends, Bupkis and I Want You Back) has been tapped to direct the project. He’s also writing the current draft based on a screenplay by Deborah Kaplan and Harry Elfont. Neal Moritz and Tania Landau from the original movie are also returning to produce. Related Stories Business Pam Abdy Laments Exits of Top Female Executives in Hollywood: "It Is Upsetting to See" Movies DC Studios' James Gunn and Peter Safran Recall Detailing Long-Term Plans to New Boss David Ellison

The 1998 pic, based on Miklós László’s 1937 Hungarian play Parfumerie, follows book superstore magnate Joe Fox (Hanks) and independent book shop owner Kathleen Kelly (Ryan) as they fall in love in the anonymity of the Internet. However, they’re both blissfully unaware that he’s trying to put her out of business. Greg Kinnear, Parker Posey, Jean Stapleton, Steve Zahn, Heather Burns and Dave Chappelle also starred in the film helmed by Nora Ephron.

Upon its release, You’ve Got Mail grossed more than $250 million at the global box office, not adjusted for inflation. It became Ephron’s highest-grossing film to date, behind Sleepless in Seattle (1993). Ryan also earned a Golden Globe nomination the following year for best actress in a motion picture — comedy or musical for her performance as Kathleen.

Orley is repped by UTA, Rise Management and Hansen, Jacobson, Teller, Hoberman, Newman, Warren, Richman, Rush, Kaller, & Gellman L.L.P.

Cognizant zet meer dan 25.000 professionals in voor de implementatie van Claude en introduceert een model voor beheerde diensten om Claude centraal te stellen in bedrijfsactiviteiten

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

TEANECK, New Jersey, 11 november 2026 /PRNewswire/ -- Cognizant (Nasdaq: CTSH) heeft vandaag de oprichting aangekondigd van de Claude Frontier Capability Group, een groep van meer dan 25.000 professionals die zich inzetten om Claude bij klanten te implementeren. De groep richt zich op een hoogwaardige uitvoering, transformatie en het succes van klanten en beschikt over de capaciteit om AI binnen de kernactiviteiten van ondernemingen van de pilotfase naar de productiefase te brengen.

Ondernemingen hebben toegang tot geavanceerde AI-technologie, maar vinden het nog steeds lastig om deze technologie om te zetten in concrete bedrijfswaarde. Cognizant heeft binnen zijn hele personeelsbestand expertise op het gebied van Claude opgebouwd. Meer dan 44.000 medewerkers in bedrijfsonderdelen en ondersteunende afdelingen zijn inmiddels opgeleid of gecertificeerd in het gebruik van Claude. De nieuwe groep is een gespecialiseerde organisatie die systemen op basis van Claude ontwikkelt en beheert binnen de bedrijfsomgeving van klanten.

Cognizant introduceert ook een model voor beheerde diensten voor Claude. Binnen dit model neemt Cognizant namens klanten het beheer van systemen op basis van Claude in productieomgevingen op zich, zodat ze zich kunnen richten op de bedrijfsresultaten die deze systemen mogelijk maken. Beide initiatieven bouwen voort op het Frontier-personeelsmodel van Cognizant, waarmee het bedrijf Frontier Certified Engineers en Frontier Business Operators opleidt die rechtstreeks door ontwikkelaars van geavanceerde AI-modellen worden gecertificeerd.

"Klanten willen een partner die de verantwoordelijkheid voor de uitvoering op zich neemt zodra AI binnen hun bedrijf wordt ingezet", aldus Ravi Kumar S, CEO van Cognizant. "We zetten meer dan 25.000 van onze medewerkers in voor Claude en nemen via beheerde diensten het beheer ervan voor onze klanten op ons. Daarmee investeren we fors in Claude en in de mogelijkheden die deze technologie ondernemingen biedt binnen hun kernactiviteiten."

"Alle ondernemingen proberen AI zo snel mogelijk in hun organisatie te integreren, maar medewerkers met de vaardigheden om AI in de praktijk binnen een bedrijf te laten functioneren, zijn steeds moeilijker te vinden", aldus Paul Smith, Chief Commercial Officer bij Anthropic. "Cognizant helpt dit tekort aan te pakken door meer dan 25.000 medewerkers in te zetten om klanten te ondersteunen bij het gebruik van Claude binnen hun hele organisatie."

Een model voor beheerde diensten voor Claude

Het model voor beheerde diensten van Cognizant ondersteunt klanten bij het gebruik van Claude in productieomgevingen. Het combineert gespecialiseerde medewerkers, toegang tot Claude op basis van licenties en het intellectuele eigendom van Cognizant, waaronder het AI Delivery Operating System. Het model biedt klanten beter voorspelbare AI-uitgaven en zorgt ervoor dat alle projecten worden uitgevoerd binnen beveiligings- en governancekaders op bedrijfsniveau, afgestemd op de specifieke vereisten van elke klant op het gebied van connectiviteit, architectuur en risicobeheer.

Vier prioriteiten, van het herontwerpen van bedrijfsactiviteiten tot AI-native softwareontwikkeling

De groep zal zich aanvankelijk richten op vier gebieden: het herontwerpen van bedrijfsdomeinen rond AI, het ontwikkelen en moderniseren van de ondersteunende systemen, het veilig en op grote schaal inzetten van AI-agenten en het integreren van AI-native softwareontwikkeling als kerncompetentie voor de dienstverlening. Voor de modernisering zet Cognizant speciaal ontwikkelde Claude-agenten in die de analyse en herstructurering van verouderde softwarecode en mainframecode versnellen. Samen zorgen deze vier gebieden ervoor dat Claude zich ontwikkelt van een experimentele technologie tot een integraal onderdeel van de bedrijfsinfrastructuur.

Deze verschuiving is al zichtbaar in de bedrijfsactiviteiten van klanten. Bij een wereldwijde farmaceutische fabrikant hebben door Cognizant ontwikkelde Claude-agenten voor de productievloer de stilstand met 35% verminderd en de tijd voor het oplossen van problemen teruggebracht van 90 tot 120 minuten naar 10 tot 15 minuten. Voor een media- en entertainmentbedrijf gebruikte Cognizant Claude om het opstellen van specificatiebladen met vereisten te automatiseren, waardoor de tijd tot marktintroductie werd verkort van 3 tot 4 weken naar ongeveer 15 minuten. Bij een Noord-Amerikaanse verzekeraar heeft automatisering op basis van Claude het opstellen van campagnebriefings en de ondersteuning bij pensioenregelingen met 40 tot 60% versneld.

Cognizant beschikt over meer dan 26.000 Claude-certificeringen en staat wereldwijd op de eerste plaats voor de certificering Claude Certified Architect: Foundations. Zoals aangekondigd in november 2025, rolt het bedrijf Claude uit onder zijn medewerkers, onder meer in ondersteunende afdelingen en teams voor engineering en dienstverlening.

Over CognizantCognizant (Nasdaq: CTSH) is een AI Builder en leverancier van technologische diensten die de kloof overbrugt tussen AI-investeringen en ondernemingswaarde door full-stack AI-oplossingen voor onze klanten te ontwikkelen. Onze diepgaande expertise op het gebied van sectoren, processen en engineering stelt ons in staat de unieke context van een organisatie te integreren in technologische systemen die het menselijk potentieel versterken, tastbare resultaten realiseren en wereldwijde ondernemingen helpen voorop te blijven lopen in een snel veranderende wereld. Meer informatie op www.cognizant.ai of via @cognizant.

Strategy’s STRC hit $100, but common stock still funded last week’s Bitcoin purchases

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The November 2–8 filing reports no STRC ATM sales, while buybacks absorbed two-thirds of the week’s MSTR proceeds.

Strategy funded its Bitcoin purchases with common-stock sales during November 2–8, even as STRC, its variable-rate preferred stock, reached $100. Strategy had said it would not issue STRC below that price, but its November 9 disclosure reported no STRC sales under its at-the-market offering program.

The allocation shows where the week’s financing went. Of $283.8 million in net proceeds from MSTR common shares, $188.3 million funded STRC repurchases, $81.9 million funded Bitcoin purchases and $13.6 million went to USD Cash, according to the weekly financing update.

That put about two-thirds of the new common-stock money into buying back preferred shares. Less than a third went into Bitcoin. STRC’s market-price recovery had yet to become reported STRC ATM financing within that week.

Strategy’s November 2–8 MSTR proceeds went mostly to repurchasing preferred shares. No STRC ATM sales were reported for the week.

STRC buybacks received 2.3 times the common-stock proceeds allocated to Bitcoin.

Covers November 2–8, 2026 only. Allocation is not a return forecast or a conclusion about later STRC issuance. USD Cash allocation is a flow, not the cash balance; future dividend savings depend on rates and declarations.

Reported net proceeds and allocations in USD millions: 188.3+81.9+13.6=283.8. Band widths use allocation/283.8: 66.34954%, 28.85835%, 4.79211%; labels rounded to one decimal. Buyback/Bitcoin ratio 188.3/81.9=2.29915, rounded to 2.3. Repurchased share count is reported.

Covers November 2–8, 2026 only. Allocation is not a return forecast or a conclusion about later STRC issuance. USD Cash allocation is a flow, not the cash balance; future dividend savings depend on rates and declarations.

STRC recorded an historical intraday high price of $100 on November 4 and an unadjusted close of $99.98. Its highs on November 5 and 6 were $99.97 and $99.84, respectively.

Thanks for signing up to The Catalyst. Look out for your first daily email. If you already receive it, you’re all set.

Strategy’s July 27 policy announcement said the company would not issue STRC below $100. It also described a long-term objective of healthy, sustained trading near that level. The issuance and dividend policies were statements of intent that could change.

Strategy receives new equity capital when it sells shares and collects proceeds. In a secondary-market trade, a buyer instead pays another investor for an existing share. The market price and the issuer’s financing proceeds therefore describe separate transactions.

For November 2–8, the reported financing came from selling 2,051,348 MSTR shares. That share-sales figure includes shares sold but not yet settled as of November 6.

CryptoSlate’s November 5 coverage described renewed STRC financing as a possibility and included an unconfirmed tracker estimate that issuance might have financed 191 BTC. The subsequent weekly disclosure reports no STRC ATM sales for the period containing November 4.

Strategy bought 1,036 BTC for $81.9 million during the week, at an average $79,012 per coin including fees and expenses. Its Bitcoin purchase disclosure expressly identifies MSTR sale proceeds as the funding source.

Bitcoin purchases absorbed 28.9% of the common-stock proceeds. STRC repurchases took 66.3%, or about 2.3 times the Bitcoin allocation, while USD Cash received 4.8%. That makes the buyback program a substantial competing use of newly raised capital.

It also changes the mix of claims on the company. Issuing MSTR expands the common-share base, while repurchasing STRC reduces preferred claims. Strategy bought back 1,887,464 STRC shares during November 2–8.

The company’s July policy described discounted repurchases as a way to reduce future preferred dividend requirements. That gives the spending an economic purpose alongside its immediate cash cost. The eventual dividend savings depend on future rates and declarations.

The policy also anticipated a greater repurchase pace at deeper discounts, tapering as STRC approached $100. It left decisions subject to liquidity, market conditions and capital-allocation priorities.

The preceding week also paired MSTR fundraising with STRC repurchases, but disclosed a different funding route. In its November 2 filing, Strategy reported $164 million in MSTR proceeds: $65.5 million funded Bitcoin purchases and $98.5 million went to USD Cash. It separately used $208.2 million of USD Cash for STRC repurchases.

For November 2–8, the company explicitly assigned MSTR proceeds directly to the buybacks. The comparison shows a change in the disclosed funding allocation, with common-stock sales financing Bitcoin purchases in both weeks.

At November 8, USD Cash stood at $146.2 million, alongside a separate $4.77 billion reserve for dividends and interest. Strategy used $21.1 million of that reserve to pay preferred dividends during the week.

The next financing development depends on reported issuer sales and the use of their proceeds. STRC’s return to $100 provided a market-price milestone; the November 2–8 allocation shows that MSTR issuance supplied the disclosed money for both Bitcoin accumulation and preferred-share repurchases.
